\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

such that
P
is stable under isomorphisms of squares.

A cd-structure
P
is called
squarrable
if for every morphism
Z\to X
,
the fiber product
Q\times_{X}Z
is representable and belongs to
P
.

We refer to
[
22
, Definition 3.3.22]
and
[
102
, Definition 2.10]
for the notions of
quasi-bounded cd-structures and regular cd-structures.

Definition 2.3.13
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
.

(1)

The
strict Nisnevich
cd-structure consists of cartesian squares of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in
\mathbf{lSch}/S
such that
g
is an open immersion,
f
is strict Nisnevich, and the morphism
f^{-1}(\underline{X^{\prime}}-g(\underline{X}))\to\underline{X^{\prime}}-g(\underline{X})
with reduced scheme structures is an isomorphism.

(2)

The
dividing
cd-structure consists of cartesian squares of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emptyset}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emptyset}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

in
\mathbf{lSch}/S
such that
f
is a dividing cover.

The
dividing Nisnevich
cd-structure
is the union of the strict Nisnevich cd-structure and dividing cd-structure.

On
\mathbf{lSch}
we define the
strict Nisnevich topology
to be the smallest topology finer than the Zariski topology and the topology associated with the strict Nisnevich cd-structure. Similarly, we define the
dividing Nisnevich topology
on
\mathbf{lSch}
as the smallest topology finer than the Zariski topology and the topology associated with the dividing Nisnevich cd-structure. Analogously, one can define the
dividing topology
as the topology associated with the dividing cd-structure.

We let
sNis
,
dNis
, and
div
be shorthand for these
topologies.

Remark 2.3.14
.

Every proper surjective étale monomorphism of schemes is an isomorphism by
[
34
, Théorème IV.17.9.1]
.
On the other hand,
Example
2.3.10
yields oodles of
proper surjective log étale monomorphisms between fs log schemes that are not isomorphisms.
The basic underlying idea behind the dividing topology is to eliminate this discrepancy.
Indeed,
every dividing cover becomes an isomorphism in the category of dividing sheaves.

The Nisnevich topology plays a fundamental role in the theory of motives, e.g., in
[
70
]
.
The strict Nisnevich topology is our log geometric generalization of the Nisnevich topology for schemes.
To our delight,
the dividing Nisnevich topology inherits the desirable properties of both the strict Nisnevich topology
and the dividing topology.

Remark 2.3.15
.

Let us offer a different perspective on the dividing topology. Elementary examples of log schemes are constructed by considering compactifications of classical schemes, that is, embeddings into proper schemes over the base. The choice of such embedding is not canonical, as we can clearly construct new compactifications from old ones by taking blow-ups with center in the boundary, which is precisely the locus where the log structure is nontrivial. The effect of imposing dividing descent in the category of log motives is to get rid of such choices as much as possible, by inverting a large class of blow-ups with center in the support of the log structure.

Example 2.3.16
.

The cartesian commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}}

is not a strict Nisnevich distinguished square,
despite the claim made to the contrary in
[
22
, Example 5.2.11]

since the morphism
\square\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is not strict.
Nevertheless, it is cocartesian in the category of log motives.
We refer to Proposition
2.5.12
for a more general statement.

We will often consider other topologies in order to construct useful motivic categories. Recall that a morphism
\theta\colon P\to Q
of integral monoids is called Kummer if it is injective and
\mathbb{Q}
-surjective (that is, for all
x\in Q
, there exists
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
nx\in\theta(P)
). A morphism of integral log schemes
X\to Y
is Kummer if the induced homomorphisms

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,f(x)}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}
are Kummer for all
x\in X
.
We refer to
[
50
, §2]
for a list of properties of Kummer morphisms and Kummer étale morphisms (that is, log étale and Kummer) of log schemes.

Definition 2.3.17
.

On
\mathbf{lSch}
, we have the following topologies.

(1)

The
strict étale topology
is generated by the families
\{u_{i}\colon U_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
such that each
u_{i}
is strict étale and
\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}\to X
is surjective.

(2)

The
Kummer étale topology
is generated by the families
\{u_{i}\colon U_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
such that each
u_{i}
is Kummer étale and
\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}\to X
is surjective.

(3)

The
log étale topology
is generated by the families
\{u_{i}\colon U_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
such that each
u_{i}
is log étale and
\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}\to X
is
universally surjective
.

(4)

The
dividing étale topology
is the smallest topology finer than both the dividing topology and strict étale topology.

Let
{s\acute{e}t}
,
{k\acute{e}t}
,
{l\acute{e}t}
, and
d{\acute{e}t}
be shorthand notations for these topologies.

According to
[
72
, Proposition 2.2.2]
, any exact surjective morphism is universally surjective.
Therefore, in (1) and (2), the morphism
\amalg_{i\in I}U_{i}\to X
is universally surjective.

Definition 2.3.18
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
and let
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
be the full subcategory of

\mathbf{lSm}/S
consisting of
Y\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
such that
\underline{Y}\in\mathbf{Sm}/S
.

Suppose
X\in\mathbf{Sm}/S
and
D
is a strict normal crossing divisor on
X
over
S
as in
[
22
, Definition 7.2.1]
.
Let
(X,D)
denote the fs log scheme whose underlying scheme is
X
and whose log structure is the compactifying log structure associated with the open immersion
X-D\to X
.
Every object of
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
arises as this form, see
[
22
, Lemma A.5.10]
and Remark
2.3.19
below.

We set
\square:=(\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty)
, where we regard the point at
\infty
as an effective Cartier divisor.

The category
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
is simpler to deal with than
\mathbf{lSm}/S
,
but it is unclear how to generalize a useful notion of
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
to an arbitrary fs log base scheme
S
.

Remark 2.3.19
.

Let
S
be a scheme.
The proof of
[
22
, Lemma A.5.9]
needs a small modification: it is claimed in loc. cit. that for a log smooth morphism of fs log schemes
f\colon X\to S
, Zariski locally on
X
,
there exists a neat chart
P
on
X
such that the induced morphism
X\to S\times\mathbb{A}_{P}
is strict smooth.
However, the claim in
[
78
, Theorem IV.3.3.1]
is étale local,
which implies that the above claim only étale locally on
X
.
To fix this,
use
[
78
, Theorem III.1.2.7(4)]
to obtain a neat chart
0\to P
of
f
Zariski locally on
X
,
which is possible since
P^{\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion free.
With this neat chart in hand,
argue as in
[
78
, Theorem IV.3.3.1]
to conclude.

Remark 2.3.20
.

The proof of
[
22
, Lemma A.11.3]
has a similar issue.
We refer to
[
83
, Proposition 3.4.1]
for the correct proof.

Proposition 2.3.21
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
.
Then the category
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
admits products.

Proof.

Suppose
(X,D),(Y,E)\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
.
The product
(X,D)\times_{S}(Y,E)
in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
can be written as
(X\times_{S}Y,X\times_{S}E+D\times_{S}Y)
, which is in
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
.
∎

Proposition 2.3.22
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}_{noeth}
.
The strict Nisnevich topology and the dividing Nisnevich topology on
\mathbf{lSm}/S
and
\mathbf{lSch}/S
are complete, quasi-bounded, and regular.

Proof.

We refer to
[
22
, Proposition 3.3.30]
.
∎

For further reference, we record a comparison result for Nisnevich distinguished squares.

Lemma 2.3.23
.

Suppose

(2.3.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

is a strict Nisnevich distinguished square.
Then

(2.3.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}-\partial Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y-\partial Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}-\partial X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X-\partial X}

is a Nisnevich distinguished square.

Proof.

Since
f
,
f^{\prime}
,
g
, and
g^{\prime}
are strict,
the square (
2.3.3
) is the pullback of (
2.3.2
) along the canonical open immersion

X-\partial X\to X
. The fact that
(
2.3.3
)
is then a Nisnevich square is obvious.
∎

Remark 2.3.24
.

The dividing Nisnevich topology is
not
subcanonical.
For example,
if
Y\to X
is a dividing cover of fs log schemes that is not an isomorphism,
then the naturally induced map
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{lSch}}(X,Y)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{lSch}}(Y,Y)
is not a bijection.
This means that the presheaf represented by
Y
is not a dividing sheaf.
Nevertheless,
for any
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
,
we will often use the notation
X
for the sheaf represented by
X
to avoid lengthy notation.
We often use the notation
\operatorname{pt}:=S
for the log base scheme.

2.4.
Unstable motivic homotopy categories

The existence of
\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]
for a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with respect to a
small set of morphisms
\mathcal{W}
is guaranteed by
[
63
, Proposition 5.5.4.15]
. It is a reflective localization in the sense of
[
65
,
Tag 02G9
]
.
Write
L_{\mathcal{W}}
for the left adjoint to the fully faithful inclusion
\mathcal{C}[\mathcal{W}^{-1}]\subseteq\mathcal{C}
.

To simplify the notation, we will often write
\mathcal{C}
instead of
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
for a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, abusively confusing
\mathcal{C}
with
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{\langle 1\rangle}
.
For abbreviation, we will also write
\tau
for the set of Čech nerves of
\tau
-covers, where
\tau
is a Grothendieck topology on the category
\mathcal{C}
. For example, write
dNis
for the set of Čech nerves of
dNis
-covers.

Notation 2.4.1
.

For an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, write
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C})
for the
\infty
-category of presheaves of spaces (or Kan complexes) on
\mathcal{C}
. When
\mathcal{C}
is endowed with a Grothendieck topology
\tau
, we write
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})\subseteq\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C})
for the full
\infty
-subcategory spanned by
\tau
-sheaves, and we write
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{C})
for the full
\infty
-subcategory spanned by
\tau
-hypersheaves in the sense of
[
63
, §6.5.4]
. There is a canonical functor
(-)^{\wedge}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{C})
(the hypercompletion). We will write
L_{\tau}(-)
for the sheafification functor.

Notation 2.4.2
.

Let
\Lambda\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{\geq 0})
be a commutative connective ring spectrum, and let
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}
be the
\infty
-category of
\Lambda
-modules. We denote by
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)=\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda})
the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{C}
-presheaves of
\Lambda
-modules and by
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)
the full
\infty
-subcategory of
\tau
-sheaves, if
\mathcal{C}
is endowed with a Grothendieck topology
\tau
.

Remark 2.4.3
.

We will mostly be interested in the case where
\Lambda=\mathrm{H}R
is the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum of an ordinary commutative ring
R
, or
\Lambda=\mathbb{S}
is the sphere spectrum (or a localization of the sphere spectrum at a prime
p
).

Definition 2.4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category, and let
\mathcal{D}
be a presentable
\infty
-category. Let
\mathcal{W}
be a set of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
. Let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
.
We say that
\mathcal{F}
is
\mathcal{W}
-local
if
\mathcal{F}(f)
is an equivalence for all
f\in\mathcal{W}
. A morphism
f\colon\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a
\mathcal{W}
-equivalence
if
\operatorname{Map}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\simeq\operatorname{Map}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G})
for every
\mathcal{W}
-local object
\mathcal{G}
.

Remark 2.4.5
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
admits small products. Then the Yoneda functor
y\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C})
can be lifted to a symmetric monoidal functor
y\colon\mathcal{C}^{\times}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C})^{\otimes}
, where the latter is equipped with the Day convolution product by
[
64
, Corollary 4.8.1.12]
. If
\mathcal{C}
is equipped with a Grothendieck topology
\tau
, then
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})
underlies a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})^{\otimes}
such that the sheafification functor
L_{\tau}
lifts to a symmetric monoidal functor.
Indeed, it is enough to notice that for every
Y\in\mathcal{C}
and Čech nerve of the
\tau
cover
\mathscr{X}\to X
with

X\in\mathcal{C}
the projection

\mathscr{X}\times Y\to X\times Y
is the Čech nerve of a
\tau
-cover too.
This is immediate from the definitions.

Remark 2.4.6
.

Let
\Lambda
be as in Notation
2.4.2
. Then there are symmetric monoidal functors

\Lambda(-)\colon\mathcal{C}^{\times}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)^{\otimes},\quad\Lambda_{\tau}(-)\colon\mathcal{C}^{\times}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)^{\otimes}.

The functor
\Lambda(-)
is simply given by sending
X\in\mathcal{C}
to the free
\Lambda
-module on
y(X)
. Formally, it is induced on the (pre)sheaves categories by the composition
\mathcal{S}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\Sigma^{\infty}}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\otimes}\xrightarrow{-\otimes\Lambda}\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}
.

Remark 2.4.7
.

(Hypercomplete version and descent by squares) Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is a category with an initial object, and let
P
be a complete and regular cd-structure with the associated topology
\tau
. Then
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{C})
is a
\tau
-sheaf if and only if
\mathcal{F}(Q)
is cartesian for every distinguished square
Q
of the form (
2.3.1
) by
[
102
, Corollary 5.10]
.

If
P
be a quasi-bounded, regular, and squarrable cd-structure,
then we have an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{C}).

If
P
were a bounded, complete, and regular cd-structure,
then this follows from
[
102
, Proposition 3.8(a),(b)]
.
In our setting, use
[
22
, Lemma 3.4.10]
instead of
[
102
, Lemma 3.5]
.

The situation is more subtle when
\tau
is not the topology associated to a cd-structure. Assume that
\Lambda
is as in Notation
2.4.2
, and assume that
\Lambda
is eventually coconnective (that is,
\pi_{i}(\Lambda)=0
for
i
large enough). Assume moreover that the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

(\mathcal{C},\tau)
has finite local
\Lambda
-cohomological dimension, and
\mathcal{C}
is an ordinary category with fiber products.

(2)

there exists a full subcategory
\mathcal{C}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
, stable under fiber products, spanned by quasi-compact objects (in the sense of
[
4
, Exposé VI, Définitions 1.1]
) of finite
\Lambda
-cohomological dimension, and such that every object of
\mathcal{C}
admits a
\tau
-cover by objects of
\mathcal{C}_{0}
.

Then every
\tau
-sheaf of
\Lambda
-modules is an hypersheaf, that is,
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)
. This is the content of
[
11
, Corollary 2.4.6]
.

A classical example of application of the above criterion is when
\mathcal{C}=\mathbf{lSm}/S
for
S
a quasi-compact and quasi-separated fs log scheme, and
\tau
is log étale or the Kummer étale, or the dividing étale, or the strict étale topology and
\Lambda
is a (classical) torsion ring. This follows from
[
73
, Theorem 7.2]
. If
\tau
is for example the strict étale topology, the same holds for the case
\Lambda
a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra (in this case the Nisnevich and the étale cohomology coincide for any discrete sheaf, since the rational Galois cohomology groups vanish in positive degree).

Construction 2.4.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an ordinary category with small limits.
Suppose
\mathcal{P}
is a class of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
closed under isomorphisms, compositions, and pullbacks.
For every morphism
f\colon Y\to X
in
\mathcal{C}
, there is a functor

(2.4.1)

\mathcal{P}/X\to\mathcal{P}/Y

sending
T\in\mathcal{P}/X
to
T\times_{X}Y
.
This naturally induces a functor

(2.4.2)

f^{*}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/X)^{\otimes}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/Y)^{\otimes}

of
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})
. We denote by
f_{*}
its right adjoint. If
f\in\mathcal{P}
, then (
2.4.1
) has a left adjoint, sending
T\in\mathcal{P}/Y
to
T
.
This naturally induces an adjunction

(2.4.3)

f_{\sharp}:\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/Y)\rightleftarrows\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/X):f^{*}.

With the above notation, suppose that
\tau
is a topology on
\mathcal{C}
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathcal{P}
.
Then the sets of
\tau
-(hyper)covers are closed under
f_{\sharp}
for all
f\in\mathcal{P}
and
f^{*}
for all morphisms
f
. By
[
63
, Proposition 5.5.4.20]
we then obtain functors

f^{*}:\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathcal{P}/X)^{\otimes}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathcal{P}/Y)^{\otimes}

of
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})
, and, if
f\in\mathcal{P}
, an adjunction

f_{\sharp}:\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}\mathcal{P}/Y)\rightleftarrows\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathcal{P}/X):f^{*},

between the corresponding categories of (hyper)sheaves.

Proposition 2.4.9
.

The assignments
X\in\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/X)^{\otimes}
and
X\in\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/X)^{\otimes}
extend to functors

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/-)^{\otimes},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/-)^{\otimes}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}),

that are
\mathcal{P}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories in the sense of Definition
2.1.2
. The same conclusion holds for the pointed version (that is, sheaves and presheaves of pointed spaces), and for the categories of (pre)sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules for any commutative ring spectrum
\Lambda
.

Proof.

This result is “classical” and formal. See e.g.,
[
9
, §1.4]
for a proof using model categories. The fact that all the assignments in sight can be promoted to functors with value in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})
is discussed in Robalo’s thesis
[
86
, Chapter 9]
.
∎

Remark 2.4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
be as in Construction
2.4.8
.
If
\mathcal{A}=\{\mathcal{A}_{X}\}_{X\in\mathcal{C}}
is a set of morphisms in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/X)
closed under
-\otimes Y
for all
Y\in\mathcal{P}/X
,
f_{\sharp}

for all
f\in\mathcal{P}
, and
f^{*}
for all morphisms
f
,
then we similarly have a
\mathcal{P}
-premotivic
\infty
-category

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/-)^{\otimes}[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})

Indeed, to construct the functor
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/-)^{\otimes}[\mathcal{A}^{-1}]
from
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathcal{P}/-)^{\otimes}
,
use
[
64
, Proposition 4.1.7.2]
,
[
12
, Proposition C.5]
, and
[
11
, Lemma 4.3.13]
.

Remark 2.4.11
.

Following
[
11
, Remark 2.1.9]
, we are ignoring set-theoretic issues when dealing with localizations of the categories of presheaves on not necessarily small categories. This problem can be circumvented, as discussed in
[
11
]
, by passing directly to the category of sheaves. We will not mention this explicitly in what follows.

We define our basic objects as follows:

Definition 2.4.12
.

Let
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
, and let
\tau
be any topology on
\mathbf{lSch}/S
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
.

We set

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)\quad\text{(resp. $\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)$)}

to be the full
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/S)
(resp. of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}_{\ast})
) spanned by the objects
M
that are local with respect to the maps
L_{\tau}(y(X\times\square))\to L_{\tau}(y(X))
for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
(resp.
L_{\tau}(y(X\times\square)_{+})\to L_{\tau}(y(X)_{+})
, where
(-)_{+}
is the functor given by adding a base point
\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{S}_{*}
). We denote by
L_{\square}
the left adjoint to the inclusion functor
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)\subset\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/S)
(and similarly for the pointed version).

Similarly, let
\Lambda
be a connective commutative ring spectrum as in Notation
2.4.2
. We set
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
to be the full
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}_{S},\Lambda)
spanned by the objects
M
that are local with respect to the maps
\Lambda_{\tau}(X\times\square)\to\Lambda_{\tau}(X)
. Write again
L_{\square}
for the left adjoint to the inclusion functor.

The category
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)
(resp.
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)
, resp.
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
) will be called the
unstable
(resp.
pointed
, resp.
effective
or
S^{1}
-stable
,
\Lambda
-linear) motivic log homotopy category of
S
.

Remark 2.4.13
.

To simplify the notation, write
\square
for the set of maps
X\times\square\to X
for
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
, and use
\square
also to denote the set of
\square
-equivalences in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\mathcal{D})
for a presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}
. Then we have equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S):=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/S)[\square^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{lSm}/S)[(\square,\tau)^{-1}],

\displaystyle\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S):=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}_{\ast})[\square^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pc}_{\ast})[(\square,\tau)^{-1}],

\displaystyle\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda):=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\Lambda)[\square^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\Lambda)[(\square,\tau)^{-1}],

where
(\square,\tau)
denotes the saturation of the union of the set of
\square
-local equivalences and of the Čech nerves of
\tau
-covers. We write
L_{\square,\tau}=L_{\square}\circ L_{\tau}
for the
(\square,\tau)
-localization functor.

Remark 2.4.14
.

When
\Lambda
is the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum of a classical (discrete) ring, the category
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
coincides with the category
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
considered elsewhere (notably in
[
19
]
,
[
18
]
). When
\Lambda=\mathbb{S}
is the sphere spectrum, we will simply write
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S)
or
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1},\tau}(S)
(for better comparison with the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant version).

We will omit the subscript
\tau
from the notation for the construction obtained using the dividing Nisnevich topology, which is the most commonly used throughout this paper.

Remark 2.4.15
.

For any
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
, we will from now on use the notation
X
and
X_{+}
for the sheaf
L_{\tau}(y(X))
and the pointed sheaf
L_{\tau}(y(X)_{+})
respectively. The adding a base-point functor
\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{S}_{*}
induces a monoidal functor

(-)_{+}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S).

Proposition 2.4.16
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)
, (resp.
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)
, resp.
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
) is presentable, and is generated under colimits by the family of
X
(resp.
X_{+}
, resp
\Sigma^{\infty}_{S^{1}}X_{+}\otimes\Lambda
and their desuspensions) for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
. Moreover,
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)
, (resp.
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)
, resp.
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
) underlies a presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, where the monoidal structure is the Day convolution of the cartesian product in
\mathbf{lSm}/S

Proof.

The fact that all the categories in sight are presentable
\infty
-categories follows from the presentability of the corresponding
\infty
-categories of
\tau
-sheaves (
[
63
, Proposition 5.5.3.6 and Remark 5.5.1.6]
), together with the fact that we are localizing with respect to a small set of morphisms. The collections of objects
L_{\tau}y(X)
(resp.
\Sigma^{\infty}_{S^{1}}X_{+}\otimes\Lambda=\Lambda_{\tau}(X)
) for
X\in\mathcal{C}
clearly generates
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})
(resp.
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C},\Lambda)
), and similarly for the pointed version.
The statement about the monoidal
\infty
-category follows from
[
64
, Proposition 2.2.1.9]
.
∎

Remark 2.4.17
.

Let

\Sigma_{S^{1}}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)

be the
S^{1}
-suspension functor, induced by
\mathcal{F}\mapsto S^{1}\wedge\mathcal{F}
for any pointed presheaf
\mathcal{F}
.

We have an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

(2.4.4)

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{S^{1}}}\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{S^{1}}}\cdots),

which exhibits
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S)
as the
S^{1}
-stabilization
\mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S))
of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)
in the sense of Remark
2.2.2
. Write
\Sigma_{S^{1},+}^{\infty}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S)
for the
S^{1}
-infinite suspension functor. On representables, it sends
X
to
\Sigma^{\infty}_{S^{1}}X_{+}
.

Note that the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
for any connective commutative ring spectrum
\Lambda
are all stable.

Remark 2.4.18
.

Observe that the tensor product operation in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(X)
for every
X\in\mathbf{lSch}
preserves small colimits
in each variable. The same applies to
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(X)
and to
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(X,\Lambda)
.

Proposition 2.4.19
.

Let
\tau
be any topology on
\mathbf{lSch}
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}
. Let
\Lambda
be a connective commutative ring spectrum as in Notation
2.4.2
. Then the assignments

S\mapsto\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)^{\otimes},\quad\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)^{\otimes},\quad\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)^{\otimes}

can be promoted into functors

\displaystyle\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(-)\colon

\displaystyle\mathbf{lSch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(-)\colon

\displaystyle\mathbf{lSch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(-,\Lambda)\colon

\displaystyle\mathbf{lSch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}))

that are
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories in the sense of Definition
2.1.2
. In particular, the following holds:

(1)

for every morphism
f\colon X\to Y
in
\mathbf{lSch}
, the functor
f^{*}
is monoidal, and has a right adjoint
f_{*}
,

(2)

for every log smooth morphism
f\colon X\to Y\in\mathbf{lSch}
, the functor
f^{*}
has a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
, and the canonical map

f_{\sharp}(f^{*}M\otimes N)\xrightarrow{\simeq}M\otimes f_{\sharp}N

is an equivalence;

(3)

for every cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where
f
is a log smooth morphism, the Beck-Chevalley transformation
f_{\sharp}^{\prime}{g^{\prime}}^{*}\to g^{*}f_{\sharp}
is an equivalence.

For every
S
as above, we denote the homotopy category of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(S)
by
\mathbf{logH}_{\tau}(S)
, and similarly write
\mathbf{logH}_{\ast}(S)
(resp.
\mathbf{logSH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
) for the pointed (resp.
\Lambda
-linear) variants.

Proof.

This is now a formal consequence of Proposition
2.4.9
and Remark
2.4.10
.
∎

Remark 2.4.20
.

We can promote the functor
(-)_{+}
of Remark
2.4.15
to a functor of
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories

(2.4.5)

(-)_{+}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}

for every topology
\tau
on
\mathbf{lSch}
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}
. Similarly, the infinite suspension functors
\Sigma^{\infty}_{S^{1},+}(-)
and
\Sigma^{\infty}_{S^{1},+}(-)\otimes\Lambda
can be promoted to a functor of
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories.

To simplify the notation, from now on we will write
X_{+}\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)
(resp.
\Lambda_{\tau}(X)\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
) for
L_{\square}L_{\tau}(y(X)_{+})
(resp.
L_{\square}\Sigma^{\infty}_{S^{1},+}(L_{\tau}(y(X)))\otimes\Lambda
).

2.5.
The Tate circle and stable motivic homotopy theory

We begin with combining the discussion in §
2.2
with the construction of our motivic categories.

Construction 2.5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
and
\tau
be as in Construction
2.4.8
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a class of morphisms in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/-)
that is closed under tensor products
-\otimes Y
for all
Y\in\mathcal{P}/-
,
f_{\sharp}
for all
f\in\mathcal{P}
, and
f^{*}
for all morphisms
f
.

Let
B
be an object of
\mathcal{P}/*
, where
*
denotes the final object of
\mathcal{C}
.
For every morphism
f\colon X\to*
in
\mathcal{C}
, we set
f^{*}B:=B
for simplicity of notation.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be any map in
\mathcal{C}
. The monoidality of the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/X)_{\ast}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/Y)_{\ast}
gives natural isomorphisms

f^{*}(B\otimes(-))\cong B\otimes f^{*}(-),

so that by the universal property of the
B
-stabilization we obtain a functor

(2.5.1)

f^{*}:\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{B}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/X)_{\ast}[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])^{\otimes}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{B}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/Y)_{\ast}[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])^{\otimes}

of presentable, stable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

Similarly, if
f\in\mathcal{P}
, the functor

f_{\sharp}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/Y)_{\ast}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/X)_{\ast}
, gives a natural equivalences

f_{\sharp}(B\otimes(-))\simeq B\otimes f_{\sharp}(-).

Hence the functor (
2.4.3
) naturally induces an adjunction

(2.5.2)

f_{\sharp}:\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{B}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/Y)_{\ast})\rightleftarrows\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{B}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/X)_{\ast}):f^{*}.

In summary, the assignment
X\mapsto\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{B}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/X)_{\ast})

can be promoted into a functor

(2.5.3)

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{B}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathcal{P}/-)_{\ast}[\mathcal{A}^{-1}])^{\otimes}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})

sending
f\colon Y\to X
to
f^{*}
.
One can show that (
2.5.3
) is a
\mathcal{P}
-premotivic
\infty
-category, using e.g.,
[
86
, §9.1]
again.

Remark 2.5.2
.

We have implicitly used this construction above for the case of
B=S^{1}
, the standard circle (that is a symmetric object, so in particular Proposition
2.2.8
applies). For
\mathcal{C}=\mathbf{lSch}
, and
\mathcal{P}=\mathbf{lSm}
and
\tau
a topology on
\mathbf{lSch}
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}
, the
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
stable

\infty
-category

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\Sigma}_{S^{1}}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/-)[\square^{-1}])^{\otimes}

is exactly
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(S)^{\otimes}
(and similarly for the
\Lambda
-linear version).

Definition 2.5.3
.

If
X_{0}\to X_{1}
is a morphism in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
,
let
X_{1}/X_{0}
be shorthand notation for
\operatorname{cofib}(X_{0}\to X_{1})
.
If
X_{0}\to X_{1}
is a morphism in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
,
let
X_{1}/X_{0}
be shorthand notation for
\operatorname{cofib}((X_{0})_{+}\to(X_{1})_{+})
.

Remark 2.5.4
.

In
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory, the notation
X_{1}/X_{0}
is often used when
X_{0}
is a subscheme of a scheme
X_{1}
.
The quotient
\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{A}^{1}
is such an example.
In
\square
-homotopy theory we replace
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by
\square
and consequently the quotient
\mathbb{P}^{1}/\mathbb{A}^{1}

gets replaced by
\mathbb{P}^{1}/\square
although
\square
is not, strictly speaking, a subscheme of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
This is the reason why we require more flexibility when using the notation
X_{1}/X_{0}
.

In
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory it is common to write
(X,x)
for a scheme
X
pointed at
x
.
For example, the Tate circle is the pointed motivic space
(\mathbb{G}_{m},1)
.
However,
the same notation refers to a log scheme in our setting.
To avoid possible confusion, we shall use the notation
X/x
.
For example,
\mathbb{P}^{1}
pointed at
1
is written as
\mathbb{P}^{1}/1
.

Definition 2.5.5
.

The
log multiplicative group scheme
is defined as

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}:=(\mathbb{P}^{1},0+\infty).

The log Tate circle
in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
is

(2.5.4)

S_{t}^{1}:=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}/1.

Morel-Voevodsky
[
70
]
write
T:=\mathbb{A}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
for the motivic Tate object.
In
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
,
we use the notation

(2.5.5)

T:=\mathbb{A}^{1}/(\mathbb{A}^{1},0).

Note that the projection
(\mathbb{A}^{1},0)\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is proper,
while the open immersion
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is not proper.

We combine Proposition
2.3.22
and Remark
2.4.7
to deduce the following two properties.

Proposition 2.5.6
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
and let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

be a strict Nisnevich distinguished square in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
.
Then this is a cocartesian square in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.

Proposition 2.5.7
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
and let
Y\to X
be a dividing cover in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
.
Then
Y\to X
is an equivalence in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.

For the theory of cubes in an
\infty
-category, we refer to Appendix
A
.
If
Q
is an
n
-cube in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
, let
Q_{+}
be the naturally induced
n
-cube in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
.

Proposition 2.5.8
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
and let
Q
be the cube associated with a Zariski cover

\{U_{i}\to X\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
.
Then, in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
, we have an equivalence

\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q))\simeq X.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.
If
n=1
, the claim is clear.
If
n>1
, let
Q_{i}
be the
(n-1)
-cube
Q|_{\{i\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-1}}
.
From (
A.0.2
), we have a cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q))}

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.
Together with the induction hypothesis, this becomes a cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{1}\cap(U_{2}\cup\cdots\cup U_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{2}\cup\cdots\cup U_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q))}

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.
Proposition
2.5.6
finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 2.5.9
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
,
X
is a scheme in
\mathbf{Sm}/S
, with a strict normal crossing divisor
Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{m}
,
and
n
is an integer in
[1,m]
.
For simplicity of notation, we set
Y:=(X,Z_{n+1}+\cdots+Z_{m})
and

Y_{I}:=(X,Z_{i_{1}}+\cdots+Z_{i_{r}}+Z_{n+1}+\cdots+Z_{m})

if
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}
is a subset of
[1,n]
.
In this setting,
let

\mathrm{cube}(Y,Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{n})\text{ and }\mathrm{horn}(Y,Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{n})

denote the cube and cubical horn naturally consisting of

Y_{I}
.
For example, if
m=3
and
n=2
, then
\mathrm{cube}(Y,Z_{1}+Z_{2})
is the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,Z_{1}+Z_{2}+Z_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,Z_{1}+Z_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,Z_{2}+Z_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,Z_{3}).}

We obtain the cubical horn by removing the lower right corner
(X,Z_{3})
.

Proposition 2.5.10
.

With the above notation, suppose
\{X-Z_{i}\to X\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
is a Zariski cover.
Then in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
, there is an equivalence

\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Y,Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{n}))\simeq Y.

Proof.

By Čech descent for the Zariski topology,
we are reduced to consider the intersections
(X-Z_{i_{1}})\cap\cdots\cap(X-Z_{i_{r}})
instead of
X
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume
i_{1}=1
.
In this intersection,
Z_{1}
becomes
0
.
This means that
Y_{\{1\}\cup I}\to Y_{I}
is an isomorphism of fs log schemes for every subset
I
of
[2,n]
.
Together with (
A.0.2
), this concludes the proof.
∎

We record the following elementary but important consequence of the above Proposition.

Proposition 2.5.11
.

For every
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
there is a cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{P}^{1},0+\infty)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{P}^{1},0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{1}}

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.

Proof.

If
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
, this is a special case of Proposition
2.5.10
.
For general
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
, apply
p^{*}
to have a required cocartesian square, where
p\colon S\to\mathbb{Z}
is the structure morphism.
∎

Proposition 2.5.12
.

For every
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
and
n\geq 1
there is a cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{Bl}}_{O}(\mathbb{A}^{n}),E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{Bl}}_{O}(\mathbb{P}^{n}),E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{n}}

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.
Here
O
is the origin of
\mathbb{A}^{n}
and
E
is the exceptional divisor.
It follows that there is an equivalence

T\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{P}^{1}/(\mathbb{P}^{1},0)

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
.

Proof.

Let
(a)
and
(b)
be the left and right squares in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{n}-O}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{Bl}}_{O}(\mathbb{A}^{n}),E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{n}-O}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathrm{Bl}}_{O}(\mathbb{P}^{n}),E)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{n}.}

Since
(a)
and the composite square are both strict Nisnevich distinguished squares, they are cocartesian in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.
By applying the pasting law,
see e.g.,
[
63
, Lemma 4.4.2.1]
,
we deduce that
(b)
is cocartesian in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.
∎

Proposition 2.5.13
.

If
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
,
then in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
there is an equivalence

\mathbb{P}^{1}/1\simeq S^{1}\wedge S_{t}^{1}.

Proof.

Follows from Example
2.5.11
since
(\mathbb{P}^{1},0)\cong(\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty)=\square
.
∎

Proposition 2.5.14
.

If
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
,
then in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
there is an equivalence

T\simeq S^{1}\wedge S_{t}^{1}

Proof.

Combine Propositions
2.5.12
and
2.5.13
, and use the equivalence
(\mathbb{P}^{1},0)\simeq\operatorname{pt}
in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.
∎

Definition 2.5.15
.

Let
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
, and let
\tau
be any topology on
\mathbf{lSch}/S
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
. Let
\Lambda
be as in Notation
2.4.2
and set
T:=\mathbb{A}^{1}/(\mathbb{A}^{1},0)
. We define

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)^{\otimes}=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\Sigma}_{T}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda))^{\otimes},

and write
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(-,\Lambda)^{\otimes}
for the corresponding
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-category given by Construction
2.5.1
.

Remark 2.5.16
.

Let
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
.
Write
\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}
for
S^{1}_{t}\wedge-
in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau,S^{1}}
or in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
.
We have equivalences of
\infty
-categories

(2.5.6)

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau,S^{1}}(S)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau,S^{1}}(S)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}\cdots),

where the equivalence is due to Proposition
2.2.8
in light of Proposition
3.2.15
below, since
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
is a symmetric object of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S)
(see Remark
3.3.3
for the reason why we can apply the previous result). In particular, the category
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S)
is a
stable

\infty
-category. The same argument applies to
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
for any commutative ring spectrum
\Lambda
, so that we have an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}\cdots),

Remark 2.5.17
.

Combine (
2.4.4
) and (
2.5.6
) to see that
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S)
is the colimit of the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{S^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{S^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{S^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{S^{1}}}

The sub-diagram consisting of the diagonals is final in the original diagram,
so we deduce the equivalence of
\infty
-categories

(2.5.7)

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{T}}\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{T}}\cdots).

It is worth pointing out that the above does not invoke Proposition
3.2.15
for
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)
;
in fact, we do not know whether it holds for
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)
.
A similar argument gives an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

(2.5.8)

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)\simeq\mathop{\rm lim}(\cdots\xrightarrow{\Omega_{T}}\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)\xrightarrow{\Omega_{T}}\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}(S)).

Remark 2.5.18
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a closed symmetric monoidal presentable stable
\infty
-category, let
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}}(-,-)
be the internal Hom functor, i.e., a right adjoint of
\otimes
.
By
[
64
, Corollary 1.4.4.6]
,
there exists a colimit preserving functor

\varphi_{\sharp}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}\to\mathcal{C}

sending the sphere spectrum
\mathbf{1}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
to the unit
\mathbf{1}\in\mathcal{C}
.
This admits a right adjoint
\varphi^{*}
.
Let
\operatorname{map}_{\mathcal{C}}(-,-)
denote the
mapping spectrum
,
which is given by

\operatorname{map}_{\mathcal{C}}(-,-):=\varphi^{*}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{C}}(-,-)\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}.

From the commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}}

we obtain a canonical equivalence of spectra

(2.5.9)

\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)}(\mathcal{F},\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}\mathcal{G})\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)}(\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})

for all
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
,
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)
, and
\mathcal{G}\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
.

Definition 2.5.19
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
.
The functors

S^{1}\wedge(-),T\wedge(-)\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)

naturally induces equivalences

\Sigma_{S^{1}},\Sigma_{T}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S).

These have right adjoints
\Omega_{S^{1}}
and
\Omega_{T}
,
which we also write as
\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{-1}
and
\Sigma_{T}^{-1}
.
By combining these functors,
we define

\Sigma^{p,q}:=\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{p-q}\Sigma_{T}^{q}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)

for all integers
p
and
q
.

Similarly, we can define
\Sigma^{p,q}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)
for all integers
q\geq 0
and
p
.

2.6.
Auxiliary models

Next we explain two classes of morphisms that we often want to invert in our log motivic homotopy categories.

Definition 2.6.1
.

Suppose that
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated, and
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
(in particular,
\underline{X}
is of finite presentation over
S
).
If
Z\in\mathbf{Sm}/S
is a closed subscheme of
\underline{X}
that has strict normal crossing with
\partial X
,
the
blow-up of
X
along
Z
, denoted

{\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}X
, is the fs log scheme whose underlying scheme is the
blow-up
{\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}\underline{X}
,
and whose log structure is the compactifying log structure associated with the open immersion

X-(\partial X\cup Z)\to{\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}\underline{X}
.
Observe that there is a canonical projection
{\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}X\to X
.

In the case of
Z\subset\partial X
,
we say that
{\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}X
is an

admissible blow-up along a smooth center
.
The smallest class of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
containing all admissible blow-ups along smooth centers
and closed under compositions is denoted by
\mathbf{SmAdm}/S
.

Lemma 2.6.2
.

The class
\mathbf{SmAdm}/S
is closed under pullbacks along arbitrary morphisms
S^{\prime}\to S
of schemes.

Proof.

By working strict étale locally on
X
,
we reduce to the case when
Z\to\underline{X}
is given by a pullback of

Z_{0}:=\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\{0\}\times\underline{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}^{n}}\times\{0\}\to\underline{X_{0}}:=\mathbb{A}^{m+m^{\prime}}\times\underline{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}^{n+n^{\prime}}}

with
n^{\prime}>0
along a composite morphism
\underline{X}\to\underline{X_{0}}\times S\to\underline{X_{0}}
,
where the first morphism is étale and the second morphism is the projection.
We set
X_{0}:=\mathbb{A}^{m+m^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}^{n+n^{\prime}}
.
We have an isomorphism

{\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\cong({\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z_{0}}X_{0}\times S^{\prime})\times_{\underline{X_{0}}\times S}\times\underline{X}

To conclude,
recall that
\underline{X}\to\underline{X_{0}}\times S
is étale and the blow-up construction is closed under flat base change.
∎

Definition 2.6.3
.

Let
\mathbf{cSch}
be the category of pairs
X=(\underline{X},X-\partial X)
of noetherian separated schemes of finite
Krull dimensions satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

X-\partial X
is a dense open subscheme of
\underline{X}
,

(ii)

the complement of
X-\partial X
in
\underline{X}
is the support of a Cartier divisor on
X
(possibly empty).

A morphism
f\colon Y\to X
in
\mathbf{cSch}
is a commutative square of schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y-\partial Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X-\partial X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f-\partial f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

We can endow
X\in\mathbf{cSch}
with the compactifying log structure associated with
X-\partial X\to\underline{X}
.
By
[
78
, Proposition III.1.6.3]
,
the said log structure is integral,
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}
is nontrivial for a point
x
of
X
if and only if
x\in X-\partial X
.
Hence, according to
[
78
, Proposition III.1.6.2]
,
it follows that
\mathbf{cSch}
is a full subcategory of log schemes.

Observe that for all morphisms
Y,Z\to X
in
\mathbf{cSch}
,
the fiber product is given by the formula

Y\times_{X}Z\simeq(\underline{Y}\times_{\underline{X}}\underline{Z},(Y-\partial Y)\times_{X-\partial X}(Z-\partial Z)).

Since
\mathbf{cSch}
admits a final object
(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}),\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}))
,

\mathbf{cSch}
admits finite limits.
We use the notation
\square:=(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathbb{A}^{1})
in
\mathbf{cSch}
in analogy with
\square
in
\mathbf{lSch}
.
The “c” in
\mathbf{cSch}
refers to the compactifying log structure.

Remark 2.6.4
.

Unlike the theory of modulus pairs developed in
[
53
]
and
[
58
]
,
a morphism
Y\to X
in
\mathbf{cSch}
does not require extra conditions on the boundaries
\partial Y
and
\partial X
.

Definition 2.6.5
.

Let
\mathbf{cSm}
denote the class of morphisms
f\colon Y\to X
in
\mathbf{cSch}
such that the morphism
f-\partial f\colon Y-\partial Y\to X-\partial X
is smooth.

The class
\mathbf{cSm}
is closed under compositions and pullbacks.
For
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
,

\mathbf{cSm}/S
contains
\mathbf{lSm}/S
as a full subcategory.

Definition 2.6.6
.

An
admissible blow-up
is a morphism
p\colon Y\to X
in
\mathbf{cSch}

such that
\underline{p}\colon\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
is proper and the naturally induced morphism of schemes

Y-\partial Y\to X-\partial X
is an isomorphism.
The class of admissible blow-ups in
\mathbf{cSch}
is denoted by
\mathbf{Adm}
.

Observe that
\mathbf{Adm}
is closed under compositions and pullbacks and
\mathbf{Adm}\subset\mathbf{cSm}
.

Definition 2.6.7
.

We say that a morphism
f\colon Y\to X
in
\mathbf{cSch}
is
strict
if the naturally induced morphism
Y-\partial Y\to(X-\partial X)\times_{X}Y
is an isomorphism.
We say that
f
is a
strict Nisnevich cover
if
f
is strict and
\underline{f}\colon\underline{Y}\to\underline{X}
is a Nisnevich cover of schemes.

Remark 2.6.8
.

When discussing a non
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant cohomology theory
H^{i}
of a scheme
X
over a field
k
,
such as Hodge cohomology,
it is often convenient to consider a compactification
Y\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/k
such that
Y-\partial Y\cong X

and
Y
is proper over
k
.
One may want to extend
H^{i}
to
\mathbf{SmlSm}/k
and take
H^{i}(Y)
as a new cohomology group of
X
,
but a technical problem of this approach is to show independence of the choice of
Y
.
To achieve this,
it is necessary to show that
H^{i}(Y)
is invariant under admissible blow-ups
Y^{\prime}\to Y
for
Y,Y^{\prime}\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/k
with
Y
proper.
This is the reason why we are considering admissible blow-ups.

Construction 2.6.9
.

Suppose that
\tau
is a topology on
\mathbf{lSch}
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}
. For every
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
as above, write

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{(\ast),\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{SmlSm}/S)_{(\ast)}[(\square\cup\mathbf{SmAdm})^{-1}]\subset\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{SmlSm}/S)_{(\ast)}

for the full
\infty
-subcategory of the
\infty
-category of (pointed)
\tau
-sheaves on
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
spanned by the objects
M
that are local with respect to the maps
L_{\tau}(X\times\square)_{(+)}\to L_{\tau}(X)_{(+)}
for all
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
and the maps
f\in\mathbf{SmAdm}/S
. Note that this construction underlies a presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{(\ast),\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)^{\otimes}
.

In a similar fashion, we can consider for every
S\in\mathbf{cSch}
the
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{Adm}}(S)\subset\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{sNis}(\mathbf{cSm}/S)

spanned by the objects
M
that are
\square
-local, and
\mathbf{Adm}
-local in the obvious sense. Again, this construction underlies a presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{Adm}}(S)^{\otimes}
, and admits a pointed variant.

The following result is a verbatim translation of the previously seen arguments.

Proposition 2.6.10
.

The assignments

S\mapsto\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)^{\otimes},\quad S\mapsto\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{(\ast),\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)^{\otimes}

can be promoted into functors

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}},\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}\colon\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})

that are
\mathbf{Sm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories. Similarly, we can define

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{Adm}},\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{Adm}}\colon\mathbf{cSch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})

that are
\mathbf{cSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories.

Remark 2.6.11
.

Any dividing cover is already contained in
\mathbf{SmAdm}
.
Hence there is a canonical equivalence of
\mathbf{Sm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{sNis}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}\simeq\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{dNis}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}.

Similar results hold for
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}
,
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{Adm}}(k)
, and
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{Adm}}(k)
.

Remark 2.6.12
.

It is not clear whether there is an equivalence of
\mathbf{Sm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}\simeq\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}.

The reason why we do not invert admissible blow-ups along smooth centers in our default construction of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}

is that we do not know how to construct
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)
for arbitrary
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
. Indeed, it
is unclear how to define
\mathbf{SmAdm}/S
when
S
has a nontrivial log structure.

Finally,
we note that in the stable setting there exists an equivalence as in Remark
2.6.12
,
see Corollary
3.5.4
.

Remark 2.6.13
.

Everything can be carried over to the stable setting.
We can construct
\mathbf{Sm}
-premotivic

\infty
-categories

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1},\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}},\;\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}\colon\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}})

and
\mathbf{cSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}^{\mathbf{Adm}},\;\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\mathbf{Adm}}\colon\mathbf{cSch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}).

2.7.
Generators and compact objects

We have already discussed a set of generators for our unstable motivic categories. We extend the results to the
T
-stable setting, and explain why, in our cases of interest, the resulting categories are in fact compactly generated.

Proposition 2.7.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category generated by a family
\mathscr{F}
, and let
B
be an object of
\mathcal{C}
.
Then the family of
\Sigma_{B}^{-i}\Sigma_{B}^{\infty}X
for all
X\in\mathscr{F}
and
i\geq 0
generates
\mathrm{Stab}_{B}(\mathcal{C})
.

Proof.

Suppose
f\colon\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
is a map in
\mathrm{Stab}_{B}(\mathcal{C})
, and we write
\mathcal{F}(X)
and
\mathcal{G}(X)
as
(\mathcal{F}_{0}(X),\mathcal{F}_{1}(X),\ldots)
and
(\mathcal{G}_{0}(X),\mathcal{G}_{1}(X),\ldots)
for
X\in\mathscr{F}
.
Then
f
is an equivalence if and only if
\mathcal{F}_{i}(X)\to\mathcal{G}_{i}(X)
is an equivalence of spaces for all
X\in\mathscr{F}
and integer
i\geq 0
.
Since
\mathcal{F}_{i}(X)\to\mathcal{G}_{i}(X)
can be written as

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Stab}_{B}(\mathcal{C})}(\Sigma_{B}^{-i}\Sigma_{B}^{\infty}X,\mathcal{F})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{Stab}_{B}(\mathcal{C})}(\Sigma_{B}^{-i}\Sigma_{B}^{\infty}X,\mathcal{G}),

we conclude.
∎

Lemma 2.7.2
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
.
Then the family of
\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}X_{+}\otimes\Lambda
for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
generates
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S,\Lambda)
under colimits, desuspensions, and negative
T
-suspensions.
A similar result holds for the
\mathbf{SmAdm}
-variants.

Proof.

Immediate from Proposition
2.7.1
using Proposition
2.4.16
.
∎

Proposition 2.7.3
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}_{noeth}
, and assume that either

(1)

\tau
is the dividing Nisnevich topology or,

(2)

\tau=d{\acute{e}t},l{\acute{e}t}
, and
\Lambda
satisfies conditions (1) and (2) of Remark
2.4.7
.

Then, for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
and integer
p
(resp. integers
p
and
q
),
\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{p}\Lambda_{\tau}(X)
(resp.
\Sigma^{p,q}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\Lambda_{\tau}(X)
) is compact in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)
(resp.
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
). In particular, the categories
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
and
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
are all compactly generated, stable, presentable
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

Under the above assumptions, the category
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
is hypercomplete. Hence by e.g.,
[
11
, Lemma 2.4.5]
,
\Lambda_{\tau}(X)\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
is compact for every
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
.

Recall now that
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
is defined as the full subcategory of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
spanned by
\square
-local objects. If
\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{colim}\mathcal{F}_{i}
is a filtered colimit of
\square
-local sheaves, we have equivalences

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\tau}(X),\operatorname{colim}\mathcal{F}_{i})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{colim}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\tau}(X),\mathcal{F}_{i})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{colim}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)}(\Lambda_{\tau}(X),\mathcal{F}_{i})

so that
\Lambda_{\tau}(X)
is compact in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
, noting that
\operatorname{colim}\mathcal{F}_{i}
is again
\square
-local. The case of
\Sigma^{p}_{S^{1}}\Lambda_{\tau}(X)
for arbitrary
p
follows immediately. Using Proposition
2.4.16
, we deduce that
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
is compactly generated.

For
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
, we only need to consider the case when
p=q=0
since
\Sigma^{p,q}
preserves small colimits.
Let
\{\mathcal{F}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a filtered system in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)
, and express each
\mathcal{F}_{i}
as a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
-spectrum in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1},\tau}(S,\Lambda)

\mathcal{F}_{i}=(\mathcal{F}_{i0},\mathcal{F}_{i1},\ldots)

For every integer
j\geq 0
, we set
\mathcal{G}_{j}:=\mathop{\rm colim}_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{ij}
.
Since
\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}
commutes with filtered colimits (since its left adjoint
\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}
preserves
\omega
-compact objects), we have an equivalence
\mathcal{G}_{j}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}\mathcal{G}_{j+1}
.
Hence we can form the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
-spectrum in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S,\Lambda)

\mathcal{G}:=(\mathcal{G}_{0},\mathcal{G}_{1},\ldots).

One can readily check that
\mathcal{G}
is indeed the filtered colimit of
\{\mathcal{F}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.
Since we have equivalences of spaces (we omit
\Lambda
for brevity)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S)}(\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}X_{+},\mathcal{G})\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S)}(\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+},\mathcal{G}_{0})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm colim}_{i\in I}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(S)}(\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+},\mathcal{F}_{i0})\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{i\in I}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(S)}(\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}X_{+},\mathcal{F}_{i}),

we conclude. In light of Lemma
2.7.2
, we get the statement about compact generations as well.
∎

Remark 2.7.4
.

A similar argument show that
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{dNis}(S)
and
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast,dNis}(S)
are compactly generated
\infty
-categories as well, for every fs noetherian log scheme
S
. A similar result holds for the
\mathbf{SmAdm}
-variants.

Remark 2.7.5
.

Let
S
be any scheme, with trivial log structure. Let
\Lambda
be a commutative connective ring spectrum. The
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(S,\Lambda)=\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{\tau}(\mathbf{lSm}/S,\Lambda)
are compactly generated if
\tau=sNis
or if
\tau={s\acute{e}t}
and
\Lambda
is eventually coconnective. This follows using the same argument of
[
11
, Proposition 2.4.20]
(see also
[
11
, Remark 2.4.23]
), by showing that in both cases that
\Lambda_{\tau}(X)
is compact for every
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
. We argument can be used to show that the construction of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}(S,\Lambda)
starting from
\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
produces a compactly generated stable
\infty
-category without noetherianity assumption. Note that this argument would break if
\Lambda=\mathbb{S}
(which is clearly not eventually coconnective).

2.8.
Abelian motivic homotopy categories

We will briefly discuss the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}^{\mathrm{eff}}
and
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}
in this section;
as explained above, these are abelian analogues of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}
and
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}
.
When
k
is a field
k
,
the homotopy category
\mathbf{logDA}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k)
of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k)
was discussed in
[
22
]
.

Definition 2.8.1
.

Suppose
\tau
is a topology on
\mathbf{lSch}
generated by a family of morphisms in
\mathbf{lSm}
, and
\Lambda
is a (discrete) commutative ring.
Let

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\tau}(-,\Lambda),\;\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}_{\tau}(-,\Lambda)\colon\mathbf{lSch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\Lambda})

be the
\Lambda
-linear,
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}^{\rm eff}(-,H\Lambda)
and
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(-,H\Lambda)
respectively, where
H\Lambda
is the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum of
\Lambda
.

Their homotopy categories are denoted by

\mathbf{logDA}^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\tau}(-,\Lambda),\;\mathbf{logDA}_{\tau}(-,\Lambda)\colon\mathbf{lSch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{Tri}^{\otimes}.

We often omit
\tau
in this notation when
\tau=dNis
.

The base change functor along
\mathbb{S}\to H\Lambda
induces a functor of

\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories

(2.8.1)

\beta_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1},\tau}(-)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}^{\mathrm{eff}}_{\tau}(-,\Lambda)

By considering
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\Sigma}(-)
on both sides, we obtain a functor of
\mathbf{lSm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories

(2.8.2)

\beta_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{\tau}(-)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}_{\tau}(-,\Lambda).

We denote the right adjoint by
\beta^{*}
,
which exists since
\beta_{\sharp}
preserves small colimits.

Definition 2.8.2
.

Suppose
\Lambda
is a commutative ring,
k
is a field, and
t=dNis
,
d{\acute{e}t}
, or
l{\acute{e}t}
.
Let
\mathbf{Psh}^{\mathrm{ltr}}(k,\Lambda)
be the category of presheaves with log transfers.
For
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
,
let
\Lambda_{{\mathrm{ltr}}}(X)
be the representable presheaf of
\Lambda
-modules in this category. We refer to
[
22
, Definition 4.1.1]
for details. We write
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}^{{\mathrm{ltr}}}(k,\Lambda)
for the stable
\infty
-category of complexes of presheaves with log transfers, defined in the obvious way.

Let
\tau
be a topology on
\mathbf{lSm}/k
compatible with log transfers (see
[
22
, §4.2]
). We denote by
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}^{{\mathrm{ltr}}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)
the stable
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}^{{\mathrm{ltr}}}(k,\Lambda)
spanned by the objects
\mathcal{F}
that are local with respect to the maps
\Lambda_{{\mathrm{ltr}}}(\mathscr{X})\to\Lambda_{{\mathrm{ltr}}}(X)
for all
\tau
-hypercovers
\mathscr{X}\to X
. See
[
22
, §4.3]
for a construction using the language of model categories.

We let

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda),\text{ and }\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)

be, respectively, the full
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}^{{\mathrm{ltr}}}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)
spanned by
\square
-local objects (in the obvious sense), and the stable
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
-symmetric spectra
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\Sigma}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\rm eff}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda))
. Both categories underlie presentable stable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

Their homotopy categories are denoted by
\mathbf{logDM}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
and
\mathbf{logDM}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)
.

For
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
,
the
motive of
X
is the object
M(X)\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}_{t}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
associated with

\Lambda_{{\mathrm{ltr}}}(X)
.
As before, we have the
\infty
-suspension functor

(2.8.3)

\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda).

We observe that the above construction of
\mathbf{logDM}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
is equivalent to the one
given in
[
22
, Definitions 5.2.1, 5.3.1]
.

Remark 2.8.3
.

We keep using the notation in Definition
2.8.2
.
According to
[
22
, §4.3]
,
there is a colimit preserving symmetric monoidal functor

(2.8.4)

\gamma_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda).

Similarly, we have the colimit preserving symmetric monoidal functor

(2.8.5)

\gamma_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}_{\tau}(k,\Lambda).

We denote the right adjoint by
\gamma^{*}
,
which exists since
\gamma_{\sharp}
preserves colimits.

3.
Properties of logarithmic motivic spaces and spectra

The fundamental properties of
effective
log motives were proven in the stable linear setting of
[
22
, §7]
.
We will generalize many of these properties to the unstable setting of log motivic spaces, and to the non-effective (that is,
T
-stable) setting.
For the sake of flexibility of our theory,
we will provide various models for
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S,\Lambda)
(at least when
S
is a classical scheme).

Compared to the analogous discussion in
[
22
]
, the main results of this section are contained in subsection
3.2
. In particular, we offer a proof of the fact that the Tate circle
T
is a symmetric object: this implies that the category of
T
-symmetric spectra is indeed a stable category, as explained in the previous section. Compared to the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant case, the proof is significantly more challenging and requires a new explicit geometric construction.

3.1.
Non-noetherian fs log schemes

In this subsection,
we apply Grothendieck’s technique
[
34
, §IV.8]
of noetherian approximation to deal with some non-noetherian fs log schemes using filtered limits. This is necessary in light of the applications discussed in
[
18
]
,
[
20
]
, where the base is typically an integral perfectoid ring.

Throughout this subsection,
we fix the following:
Let
S:=\operatorname{lim}_{i\in I}S_{i}
be a filtered limit of schemes such that for every morphism
i\to j
,
the morphism
S_{j}\to S_{i}
is affine.
Assume that
I
has a final object
0
and
S_{0}
is quasi-compact quasi-separated.

Example 3.1.1
.

Assume that
S=\operatorname{Spec}(A)
for some ring
A
.
Then
A
is isomorphic to a colimit of its subrings
A_{i}
that are
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras of finite type.
In this case,
we have
S\cong\operatorname{lim}_{i\in I}S_{i}
with
S_{i}:=\operatorname{Spec}(A_{i})
,
and this limit satisfies the above condition.

Definition 3.1.2
.

Suppose
W\to X
is a closed immersion in
\mathbf{Sm}/S
for
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
,
and
Z
is a strict normal crossing divisor on
X
over
S
.
We say that
W
has strict normal crossing with
Z
over
S

if Zariski locally on
X
, there exists a commutative diagram

(3.1.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]/(x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{s}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]/(x_{1}\cdots x_{r}))}

of cartesian squares for some
0\leq r\leq n
and
1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{s}\leq n
.
Here the right vertical morphisms are the obvious closed immersions,
and the horizontal morphisms are étale.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Let
X_{0}\to S_{0}
be a finitely presented morphism of schemes, and we set
X:=X_{0}\times_{S_{0}}S
.
If
f\colon U\to X
is a Zariski (resp. étale) cover such that it is finitely presented,
then there exists
i\in I
and a Zariski (resp. étale) cover
f_{i}\colon U_{i}\to X_{i}
such that it is finitely presented and
f\cong f_{i}\times_{S_{i}}S
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
[
34
, Théorèmes IV.8.8.2, IV.8.10.5(iii),(iv), Proposition IV.17.7.8]
.
∎

Proposition 3.1.4
.

For every
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
,
there exists
i\in I
and
X_{i}\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S_{i}
such that
S\times_{S_{i}}X_{i}\cong X
.
If
W\to\underline{X}
is a closed immersion in
\mathbf{Sm}/S
such that
W
has strict normal crossing with
\partial X
over
S
,
then we may further assume that there exists a closed immersion
W_{i}\to\underline{X_{i}}
in
\mathbf{Sm}/S_{i}
such that
W\to\underline{X}
is its pullback along
S\to S_{i}
and
W_{i}
is strict normal crossing with
\partial X_{i}
over
S
.

Proof.

We set
Z:=\partial X
.
By
[
34
, Théorème IV.8.8.2]
,
there exists
i\in I
and morphisms
Z_{i},W_{i}\to\underline{X_{i}}
of schemes over
S_{i}
such that
W\to\underline{X}\leftarrow Z
is the pullback of
W_{i}\to\underline{X_{i}}\leftarrow Z_{i}
.
We need to show that there exists a morphism
j\to i
in
I
such that the pullback
W_{j}\to\underline{X_{j}}\leftarrow Z_{j}
along
S_{j}\to S_{i}
satisfies the desired property.
This question is Zariski local on
X
by Lemma
3.1.3
,
so we may assume that there exists a commutative diagram
Q
of the form (
3.1.1
) for
W\to\underline{X}\leftarrow Z
.

By
[
34
, Théorème IV.8.8.2]
again,
there exists
j\to i
and a commutative diagram

Q_{j}:=\hbox to216.56pt{\vbox to71.04pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 108.28009pt\lower-35.52075pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope

of cartesian squares such that
Q\cong Q_{j}\times_{S_{j}}S
.
We may further assume that the horizontal morphisms of
Q_{j}
are étale using
[
34
, Proposition IV.17.7.8]
,
which finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 3.1.5
.

For every vector bundle
p\colon\mathcal{E}\to X
with
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
,
there exists
i\in I
and a vector bundle
p_{i}\colon\mathcal{E}_{i}\to X_{i}
with
X_{i}\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S_{i}
such that
p\cong p_{i}\times_{S_{i}}S
.

Proof.

We get
i\in I
and
X_{i}\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S_{i}
by Proposition
3.1.4
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be the locally free sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X}}
-modules corresponding to the vector bundle
\underline{p}\colon\underline{\mathcal{E}}\to\underline{X}
.
By
[
34
, Théorème IV.8.5.2, Proposition IV.8.5.5]

there exists a morphism
j\to i
in
I
and a locally free sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{j}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\underline{X_{j}}}
-modules such that
\mathcal{F}
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\mathcal{F}_{j}
.
Let
\underline{\mathcal{E}_{j}}\to\underline{X_{j}}
be the corresponding vector bundle.
Then the pullback
\mathcal{E}_{j}:=\underline{\mathcal{E}_{j}}\times_{\underline{X_{j}}}X_{j}\to X_{j}
satisfies the desired property.
∎

Proposition 3.1.6
.

For every
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
,
there exists
i\in I
and
X_{i}\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
such that
S\times_{S_{i}}X_{i}\cong X
.
If
f\colon Y\to X
is a morphism in
\mathbf{lSm}/S
,
then there exists
i\in I
and a morphism
f_{i}\colon Y_{i}\to X_{i}
in
\mathbf{lSm}/S_{i}
such that
f\cong f_{i}\times_{S_{i}}S
.
If
f
satisfies one of the following properties:

(1)

log étale,

(2)

log smooth,

(3)

log étale monomorphism,

(4)

dividing cover,

then we may further assume that
f_{i}
has the same property.

Proof.

The case (4) is a consequence of the case (3) and
[
34
, Théorème IV.8.10.5,(vi),(xii)]
.

For the cases (1)–(3),
we set
U:=X-\partial X
and
V:=Y-\partial Y
.
