\mathbf{E}^{**}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}[[c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}]]\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r))

and

(7.4.21)

\mathbf{E}^{**}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}[[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots]]\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}})

sending
c_{i}
to
c_{i}(\mathcal{T}_{r})
and
c_{i}(\mathcal{T})
,
respectively.

Proof.

Use the map
\beta
in (
7.4.6
) to form an infinite sequence

\cdots\to R_{r,n+1}\to R_{r,n}\to\cdots\to R_{r,r}.

This is isomorphic to the infinite sequence of singular cohomology rings of complex Grassmannians

\cdots\to H^{*}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r,\mathbb{C}^{n+1}))\to H^{*}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r,\mathbb{C}^{n}))\to\cdots\to H^{*}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r,\mathbb{C}^{r})).

The classical computation of the singular cohomology rings of complex Grassmannians shows that

\mathop{\rm lim}_{n}H^{*}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r,\mathbb{C}^{n}))\cong\mathbb{Z}[[c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}]]\text{ and }\mathop{\rm lim^{1}}_{n}H^{*}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r,\mathbb{C}^{n}))=0.

Hence we have

(7.4.22)

\mathop{\rm lim}_{n}R_{r,n}\cong\mathbb{Z}[[c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}]]\text{ and }\mathop{\rm lim^{1}}_{n}R_{r,n}=0.

Applying Theorem
7.4.6
and Lemma
7.4.8
we deduce (
7.4.20
)
and hence (
7.4.21
).
∎

Remark 7.4.10
.

Panin, Pimenov, and Röndigs proved Corollary
7.4.9
in the setting of

\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory,
see
[
80
, Theorem 2.2]
.
Their proof uses certain affine bundles of flag varieties,
whose compactifications can be quite complicated.
This is the reason why we have followed the argument in
[
75
]
.

7.5.
Formal group laws

Throughout this subsection, we fix
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
and an oriented homotopy commutative monoid
\mathbf{E}
in
\mathbf{logSH}(S)
.
In this section,
we will define the associated formal group law of
\mathbf{E}
and specialize in the example of
\mathbf{logKGL}
.

Definition 7.5.1
.

Consider the Segre map

(7.5.1)

m\colon\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\to\mathbb{P}^{\infty}

sending
([x_{0}:x_{1}:\cdots],[y_{0}:y_{1}:\cdots])
to
[x_{0}y_{0}:x_{0}y_{1}:x_{1}y_{0}:\cdots]
.
Together with Remark
7.4.7
and (
7.4.20
), we have a homomorphism

m\colon\mathbf{E}^{**}[[x]]\to\mathbf{E}^{**}[[x,y]].

We set
\mathrm{F}_{\mathbf{E}}(x,y):=m(x)
, which we call the
formal group law of
\mathbf{E}
.

Definition 7.5.2
.

We say that
\mathbf{E}
has a
multiplicative orientation
if there exists
an invertible element
u\in\mathbf{E}^{-2,-1}
such that

\mathrm{F}_{\mathbf{E}}(x,y)=x+y-uxy.

Example 7.5.3
.

Suppose
k
is a perfect field admitting resolution of singularities.
Theorem
6.5.7
and
[
81
, Corollary 1.2.7]
give natural isomorphisms

(7.5.2)

\mathbf{logKGL}^{p,q}(X)\cong\mathrm{K}_{2q-p}(X-\partial X)\cong{\mathbf{KGL}}^{p,q}(X-\partial X)

for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
and integers
p
,
q
.
According to
[
81
, §2.7]
,
{\mathbf{KGL}}
is oriented in the sense of
[
80
, Definition 1.2]
.
Via (
7.5.2
), the orientation on
{\mathbf{KGL}}
induces an orientation on
\mathbf{logKGL}
.

Furthermore, there is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{KGL}}^{**}(\mathrm{B}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{KGL}}^{**}(\mathrm{B}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times\mathrm{B}{\mathbb{G}}_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logKGL}^{**}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logKGL}^{**}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}\times\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Together with
[
92
, Example 2.2]
,
we see that the formal group law on
\mathbf{logKGL}
is given by

(7.5.3)

\mathrm{F}_{\mathbf{logKGL}}(x,y)=x+y-\beta xy,

where
\beta\in\mathbf{logKGL}^{-2,-1}
is the element corresponding to
{\mathbf{KGL}}
’s Bott element.
This shows that
\mathbf{logKGL}
has a multiplicative orientation.

7.6.
Logarithmic cobordism and orientations

Throughout this subsection, we fix
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
.
First,
we construct the logarithmic cobordism spectrum
{\mathbf{logMGL}}
over
S
following Voevodsky’s approach to
algebraic cobordism in
[
98
, §6.3]
,
which in turn follows that for complex cobordism. Theorem
7.6.11
establishes a one-to-one correspondence between ring homomorphisms

{\mathbf{logMGL}}\to\mathbf{E}
and orientations on any homotopy commutative monoid
\mathbf{E}
in
\mathbf{logSH}(S)

— the logarithmic version of an analogous result for algebraic cobordism shown in

[
48
]
,
[
80
]
,
[
97
]
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{r,n}
and
\mathcal{T}_{r}
denote the tautological bundle on the Grassmannian
{\mathrm{Gr}}(r,n)
and
{\mathrm{Gr}}(r)
,
respectively.
The standard embedding
\mathcal{O}^{n}\to\mathcal{O}^{n}\oplus\mathcal{O}
gives a closed immersion
{\mathrm{Gr}}(r,n)\to{\mathrm{Gr}}(r,n+1)
,
and thus a morphism of Thom spaces

(7.6.1)

\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r,n})\to\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r,n+1})

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)
.
From this, we define

{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}:=\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r}):=\mathop{\rm colim}_{n}\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r,n}).

There is a closed immersion

(7.6.2)

\mu_{r,s}\colon{\mathrm{Gr}}(r,rn)\times{\mathrm{Gr}}(s,sn)\to{\mathrm{Gr}}(r+s,(r+s)n)

sending a pair of
V\subset\mathcal{O}^{rn}
and
W\subset\mathcal{O}^{sn}
to
\alpha(V\oplus W)\subset\mathcal{O}^{(r+s)n}
,
where
\alpha\colon\mathcal{O}^{rn}\oplus\mathcal{O}^{sn}\to\mathcal{O}^{(r+s)n}
is the isomorphism shuffling the coordinates
in the sense of

(V_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{n})\oplus(W_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus W_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}V_{1}\oplus W_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{n}\oplus W_{n}

with
V_{i}\simeq\mathcal{O}^{r}
and
W_{i}\simeq\mathcal{O}^{s}
for
i=1,\ldots,n
.
Since
\mu_{r,s}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{r+s,(r+s)n})\cong\mathcal{T}_{r,rn}\times\mathcal{T}_{s,sn}
, Proposition
3.2.16
gives a map

(7.6.3)

\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r,rn})\wedge\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{s,sn})\to\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r+s,(r+s)n})

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)
.
The induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r,rn})\wedge\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{s,sn})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r+s,(r+s)n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r,r(n+1)})\wedge\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{s,s(n+1)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r+s,(r+s)(n+1)})}

commutes,
where the vertical morphisms are obtained from (
7.6.1
).
Taking colimits in (
7.6.3
) yields another map in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)
,
namely

(7.6.4)

{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}\wedge{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{s}\to{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r+s}.

The closed immersion
{\mathrm{Gr}}(s,s)\to{\mathrm{Gr}}(s,sn)
gives a map

\mathrm{Th}(\mathbb{A}^{s})\simeq\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{s,s})\to\mathrm{Th}({\mathrm{Gr}}(s,sn)).

Proposition
3.2.10
gives an equivalence
T^{s}\simeq\mathrm{Th}(\mathbb{A}^{s})
in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)
.
Together with (
7.6.3
), we have a map

\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r,rn})\wedge T^{s}\to\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r+s,(r+s)n})

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)
.
Taking colimits yields another map in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(S)
,
namely

(7.6.5)

{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}\wedge T^{\wedge s}\to{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r+s}.

Definition 7.6.1
.

Thanks to (
2.5.8
), we can define

{\mathbf{logMGL}}:=({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{0},{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{1},\ldots)\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)

whose bonding maps are given by (
7.6.5
) when
s=1
.
This is called the
logarithmic cobordism spectrum
over
S
.
In
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
, there is a canonical equivalence

(7.6.6)

{\mathbf{logMGL}}\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{r}\Sigma^{-2r,-r}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}.

Recall the functor of
\mathbf{Sm}
-premotivic
\infty
-categories
\lambda_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{MS}\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}
in Construction
4.0.18
.

Proposition 7.6.2
.

There is an equivalence in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)

\lambda_{\sharp}({\mathbf{MGL}})\simeq{\mathbf{logMGL}}.

In particular,
we can impose a commutative monoid structure on
{\mathbf{logMGL}}
.

Remark 7.6.3
.

The existence of
\lambda_{\sharp}
gives us a quick way to equip our spectrum
{\mathbf{logMGL}}
with the structure of a commutative monoid.
We remark, however, that this fact and Theorem
7.6.11
below

are not necessary
to apply the results of §
7.1
-
7.5
. In particular, the results in
[
18
]
,
[
19
]
,
[
20
]
are independent from
[
1
]
.

Proof.

Since
\lambda_{\sharp}
preserves colimits and
{\mathrm{Gr}}(n)
is a colimit of smooth schemes,
the equivalence
\lambda_{\sharp}(\Sigma^{\infty}X_{+})\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}X_{+}
for
X\in\mathbf{Sm}/S
yields an equivalence

\lambda_{\sharp}(\Sigma^{\infty}{\mathrm{Gr}}(n))\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}{\mathrm{Gr}}(n).

Furthermore, compare Proposition
3.2.19
with the definition of Thom spaces in
[
1
, §3]
to show that
\lambda_{\sharp}
preserves Thom spaces.
Together with
[
1
, Proposition 7.1]
,
we obtain the desired equivalence.
This impose a commutative monoid structure on
{\mathbf{logMGL}}
since
{\mathrm{MGL}}\in\mathbf{MS}_{S}
is a commutative monoid and
\lambda_{\sharp}
is symmetric monoidal.
∎

From now on
\mathbf{E}
is a homotopy commutative monoid in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
.

Proposition 7.6.4
.

If
\mathbf{E}
is oriented,
then there is an isomorphism

\mathbf{E}^{*+2r,*+r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r})\cong\mathbf{E}^{**}[[c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}]].

Proof.

Combine Theorem
7.3.8
and Lemma
7.4.8
to have an exact sequence

0\to\mathbf{E}^{**}\otimes\mathop{\rm lim^{1}}_{n}R_{r,n}\to\mathbf{E}^{*+2r,*+r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r})\to\mathbf{E}^{**}\otimes\mathop{\rm lim}_{n}R_{r,n}\to 0.

Apply (
7.4.22
) to conclude.
∎

Proposition 7.6.5
.

If
\mathbf{E}
is oriented,
then there are isomorphisms

\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathbf{logMGL}})\cong\mathop{\rm lim}_{r}\mathbf{E}^{*+2r,*+r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r})\cong\mathop{\rm lim}_{r}\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r))\cong\mathbf{E}^{**}[[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots]].

Proof.

There is a commutative diagram with vertical isomorphisms

(7.6.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r+1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{*+2r+2,*+r+1}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r+1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{*+2r+2,*+r+1}(\Sigma^{2,1}{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{*+2r,*+r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\smile t(\mathcal{T}_{r+1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\smile t(\mathcal{T}_{r}\oplus\mathcal{O})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\smile t(\mathcal{T}_{r})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

This naturally induces isomorphisms

\mathop{\rm lim}_{r}\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r))\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathop{\rm lim}_{r}\mathbf{E}^{*+2r,*+r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r})

and

\mathop{\rm lim^{1}}_{r}\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r))\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathop{\rm lim^{1}}_{r}\mathbf{E}^{*+2r,*+r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}).

To finish the proof, apply Lemma
7.4.8
and Corollary
7.4.9
.
∎

Proposition 7.6.6
.

If
\mathbf{E}
is oriented,
then there are isomorphisms

\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}\wedge{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{s})\cong\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r)\wedge{\mathrm{Gr}}(s))\cong\mathbf{E}^{**}[[c_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{r}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,c_{s}^{\prime\prime}]]

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathbf{logMGL}}\wedge{\mathbf{logMGL}})

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\rm lim}_{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}\wedge{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{s})

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\rm lim}_{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r)\wedge{\mathrm{Gr}}(s))\cong\mathbf{E}^{**}[[c_{1}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime\prime},\ldots]].

Proof.

Argue as in Propositions
7.6.4
and
7.6.5
, but use (
7.4.19
).
∎

Proposition 7.6.7
.

If
\mathbf{E}
is oriented,
then the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r+s))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r)\wedge{\mathrm{Gr}}(s))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r+s}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r})\wedge\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{s}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\smile t(\mathcal{T}_{r+s})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\smile(t(\mathcal{T}_{r})\wedge t(\mathcal{T}_{s}))}

commutes, where the horizontal maps are naturally induced by (
7.6.2
) and (
7.6.4
).

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
7.3.21
.
∎

Proposition 7.6.8
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}
is oriented.
With respect to the isomorphisms in
Propositions
7.6.5
and
7.6.6
,
the map

\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathbf{logMGL}})\to\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathbf{logMGL}}\wedge{\mathbf{logMGL}}),

naturally induced by the multiplication map on
{\mathbf{logMGL}}
,
sends any power series
f(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots)
to
f(d_{1},d_{2},\ldots)
for

d_{m}:=\sum_{i=0}^{m}c_{i}c_{m-i}^{\prime}

for all integer
m\geq 1
.

Proof.

By Proposition
7.6.7
, it suffices to show that the map

\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r+s,n+l))\to\mathbf{E}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r,n)\wedge{\mathrm{Gr}}(s,l))

sends
c_{m}(\mathcal{T}_{r+s,n+l})
to
\sum_{i=0}^{m}c_{i}(\mathcal{T}_{r,n})c_{n-i}(\mathcal{T}_{s,l})
.
This follows from Proposition
7.3.16
.
∎

Proposition 7.6.9
.

The logarithmic cobordism spectrum
{\mathbf{logMGL}}
is oriented.

Proof.

By (
7.6.6
), we have a morphism

(7.6.8)

\Sigma^{-2,-1}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1})=\Sigma^{-2,-1}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{logMGL}}.

This gives a class
t_{\infty}\in{\mathbf{logMGL}}^{2,1}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))
.
When
r=0
and
s=1
,
the morphism (
7.6.5
) becomes
g\colon T\to\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1})
,
which is obtained by restricting
\mathcal{T}_{1}
to the distinguished point.
The unit map
\mathbf{1}\to{\mathbf{logMGL}}
is the composite of
\Sigma^{-2,-1}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}g
and (
7.6.8
).
It follows that the restriction of
t_{\infty}
to
T
is
T\wedge\mathbf{1}
, and thus
t_{\infty}
is a Thom orientation.
∎

Proposition 7.6.10
.

Suppose that
f\colon\mathbf{E}\to\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
is a map of homotopy commutative monoids in
\mathbf{logSH}(S)
.
If
\mathbf{E}
is oriented, then
f
induces an orientation on
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Suppose
t_{\infty}\in\mathbf{E}^{2,1}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))
is a Thom orientation.
The naturally induced homomorphism
\mathbf{E}^{2,1}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))\to\mathbf{E}^{\prime 2,1}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))

sends
t_{\infty}
to some class
f(t_{\infty})\in\mathbf{E}^{\prime 2,1}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))
.
The restriction of
t_{\infty}
to
T
is
T\wedge\mathbf{1}
.
Since
f
is a ring homomorphism, the restriction of
f(t_{\infty})
to
T
is
T\wedge\mathbf{1}
.
Hence
f(t_{\infty})
is a Thom orientation.
∎

Theorem 7.6.11
.

There is a one-to-one correspondence between the set of ring maps
{\mathbf{logMGL}}\to\mathbf{E}

and the set of orientations of
\mathbf{E}
.

Proof.

Due to Proposition
7.1.11
, we only need to deal with Thom orientations.
Let
f\colon{\mathbf{logMGL}}\to\mathbf{E}
be a ring map in
\mathbf{logSH}(S)
.
By Propositions
7.6.9
and
7.6.10
,
there is a naturally induced Thom orientation
\psi(f)\in\mathbf{E}^{2,1}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))
.
Observe that
\psi(f)
is the composite of

(7.6.9)

\Sigma^{-2,-1}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1})\to{\mathbf{logMGL}}\xrightarrow{f}\mathbf{E},

where the first map is (
7.6.8
).

On the other hand,
suppose that
t_{\infty}\in\mathbf{E}^{2,1}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))
is a Thom orientation.
Then the associated Thom classes
t(\mathcal{T}_{r})\in\mathbf{E}^{2r,r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r})
are
compatible with the maps

\mathbf{E}^{2r+2,r+1}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r+1})\to\mathbf{E}^{2r,r}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r})
since
(
7.6.7
) commutes.
Proposition
7.6.5
furnishes
a class of
\mathbf{E}^{00}({\mathbf{logMGL}})
,
which yields a map
\varphi(t_{\infty})\colon{\mathbf{logMGL}}\to\mathbf{E}
.
Let us show that
\varphi(t_{\infty})
is a ring map.
In effect, we need to show the diagram in
\mathbf{logSH}(S)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{logMGL}}\wedge{\mathbf{logMGL}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{logMGL}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}\wedge\mathbf{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi(t_{\infty})\wedge\varphi(t_{\infty})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi(t_{\infty})}

commutes,
or equivalently that
\varphi(c_{\infty})
maps to
\varphi(c_{\infty})\wedge\varphi(c_{\infty})
under

\mathbf{E}^{00}({\mathbf{logMGL}})\to\mathbf{E}^{00}({\mathbf{logMGL}}\wedge{\mathbf{logMGL}}).

Using Propositions
7.6.4
and
7.6.5
, we reduce to showing

\mathbf{E}^{2r+2s,r+s}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r+s})\to\mathbf{E}^{2r+2s,r+s}({\mathrm{logMGL}}_{r}\wedge{\mathrm{logMGL}}_{s})

maps
t(\mathcal{T}_{r+s})
to
t(\mathcal{T}_{r})\wedge t(\mathcal{T}_{s})
.
This follows from Proposition
7.3.21
.

It remains to check
\varphi(\psi(f))=f
and
\psi(\varphi(t_{\infty}))=t_{\infty}
.
When
t_{\infty}
is given, the Thom classes
t(\mathcal{T}_{r})
naturally induce
\varphi(t_{\infty})
.
From the formulation (
7.6.9
), we have
\psi(\varphi(t_{\infty}))=t(\mathcal{T}_{1})
.
Together with Proposition
7.3.7
, we have
\psi(\varphi(t_{\infty}))=t_{\infty}
.

Given a map
f
we may compose with

\Sigma^{-2r,-r}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r})\to{\mathbf{logMGL}}

to define
a_{r}\in\mathbf{E}^{2r,r}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r}))
.
On the other hand,
a_{1}=\psi(f)
defines Thom classes
t(\mathcal{T}_{r})
.
By Proposition
7.6.5
,
the classes
a_{r}
and
t(\mathcal{T}_{r})
naturally induce
f
and
\varphi(\psi(f))
,
respectively.
Therefore, it suffices to show
a_{r}=t(\mathcal{T}_{r})
for every integer
r\geq 1
.
Since
f
is assumed to be a ring map, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{logMGL}}^{\wedge r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{logMGL}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\wedge r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\wedge r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

commutes.
Thus
a_{r}
maps to
a_{1}^{\wedge r}
under

(7.6.10)

\mathbf{E}^{2r,r}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r}))\to\mathbf{E}^{2r,r}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1})^{\wedge r}).

Owing to Proposition
7.3.21
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma^{-2,-1}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{1}))^{\wedge r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-2r,-r}\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{T}_{r})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}}

commutes, and (
7.6.10
) sends
t(\mathcal{T}_{r})
to
t(\mathcal{T}_{1})^{\wedge r}
.
Since
a_{1}=t(\mathcal{T}_{1})
,
in order to conclude
a_{r}=t(\mathcal{T}_{r})
it suffices to show that (
7.6.10
) is injective.

As in Proposition
7.6.8
, (
7.6.10
) is isomorphic to the map

\mathbf{E}^{**}[[c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}]]\to\mathbf{E}^{**}[[x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}]]

sending any power series
f(c_{1},\ldots,c_{r})
to
f(d_{1},\ldots,d_{r})
, where

d_{i}=\sum_{j_{1}<\cdots<j_{i}}x_{j_{1}}\cdots x_{j_{i}}

for
i=1,\ldots,r
.
This description implies that (
7.6.10
) is injective.
∎

7.7.
The log motivic Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum

Throughout this subsection,
k
is a field and
\Lambda
is a commutative ring.
We set out to define the logarithmic analogue
\mathbf{logM\Lambda}
of the motivic Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum
introduced in
[
98
]
.
We show that
\mathbf{logM\Lambda}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
.
Assuming that
k
is a perfect field admitting resolution of singularities,
we will show that
\mathbf{logM\Lambda}
is oriented.

Recall from (
2.8.3
) the infinite suspension functor

\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda).

For
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
, we define

M(X):=\Sigma_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}M(X)\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda),

where on the right-hand side,

M(X)
denotes the log motive of
X
in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
,
see Definition
2.8.2
.
The permutation

(123)\colon M(k)(1)^{\otimes 3}\to M(k)(1)^{\otimes 3}

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)
is equivalent to the identity by Proposition
3.2.15
and Theorem
3.3.1
.
If
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
, then
M(X)
is compact in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)

[
22
, Remark 5.2.6]
.
Thus by
[
8
, Théorèmes 4.3.61, 4.3.79]
,
we obtain a canonical equivalence of spaces

(7.7.1)

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X)(m),M(Y))\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{n\to\infty}\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k,\Lambda)}(M(X)(m+n),M(Y)(n))

for all
X,Y\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The functor
\omega\colon\mathbf{lSm}/k\to\mathbf{Sm}/k
naturally induces an adjunction

\omega_{\sharp}:\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda)\rightleftarrows\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda):\omega^{*}.

The functor
\omega_{\sharp}
is monoidal and sends
M(X)
to
M(X-\partial X)
for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
.

Example 7.7.1
.

For every integer
j
, let
\Omega^{j}
be the presheaf on
\mathbf{lSm}/k
given by
\Omega^{j}(X):=\Omega_{X/k}^{j}(X)
.
We set
\Omega^{j}(X):=0
if
j<0
.
According to
[
22
, Theorem 9.7.1]
, we can regard
\Omega^{j}
as an object of
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k)
such that

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}(M(X),\Omega^{j}[n])\cong H_{Zar}^{n}(X,\Omega^{j})\cong H_{sNis}^{n}(X,\Omega^{j})

for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By
[
39
, Properties 2.3(b)]
, there is an exact sequence of sheaves

0\to\Omega_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}/k}^{j}\to\Omega_{X\times\square/k}^{j}\to a_{*}\Omega_{X/k}^{j-1}\to 0

for all
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/k
, where
a\colon X\to X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the closed immersion at
\infty
.
Apply
\mathbb{R}_{Zar}\Gamma
to this sequence and use the strict
\square
-invariance of
\Omega^{j}
in

[
22
, Corollary 9.2.2]
to obtain a cofiber sequence

(7.7.2)

\mathbb{R}_{Zar}\Gamma(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\Omega^{j})\to\mathbb{R}_{Zar}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{j})\to\mathbb{R}_{Zar}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{j-1})

for all
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/k
.
Due to
[
22
, Corollary 9.2.5, Proposition A.10.2]
,
(
7.7.2
) extends to a cofiber sequence for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
.
Hence we have an equivalence in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(k)

(7.7.3)

\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}\Omega^{j}\simeq\Omega^{j-1}.

Use this as bonding maps to define the
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
-spectrum

(7.7.4)

\mathbf{\Omega}^{j}:=(\Omega^{j},\Omega^{j+1},\ldots)\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k).

We have a canonical isomorphism

(7.7.5)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k)}(M(X)(i),\mathbf{\Omega}^{j}[n])\cong H_{Zar}^{n}(X,\Omega^{j-i})

for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
and integers
i
,
j
,
n
.
This shows that
\mathbf{\Omega}^{j}
is not
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local.
Hence
\mathbf{\Omega}^{j}
is not in the essential image of
\omega^{*}
.

Proposition 7.7.2
.

Suppose
k
is a perfect field admitting resolution of singularities.
For all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
with
X
proper over
k
, the naturally induced map

M(X)\to\omega^{*}\omega_{\sharp}M(X)

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We need to show that the naturally induced map

(7.7.6)

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y)(m),M(X))\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda)}(M(Y-\partial Y)(m),M(X-\partial X))

is an equivalence of spaces for all
Y\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
.
Combine
[
22
, Theorem 8.2.11]
and (
7.7.1
) to show this.
∎

Remark 7.7.3
.

One can ask whether or not Proposition
7.7.2
holds for
\mathbf{logSH}
.
This is a question about expressing

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)}(M(Y)(m),M(X))

in terms of mapping spaces in
\mathcal{SH}(k)
for all
X,Y\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
.

Proposition 7.7.4
.

Suppose
k
is a perfect field admitting resolution of singularities.
Then the functor
\omega^{*}\colon\mathcal{DM}(k)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k)
is monoidal.

Proof.

Note that
\omega^{*}
is lax monoidal since it is right adjoint to the monoidal functor
\omega_{\sharp}
.
To show
\omega^{*}
is monoidal, it suffices to show that the map

(7.7.7)

\omega^{*}M(X)\otimes\omega^{*}M(Y)\to\omega^{*}M(X\times Y)

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{DM}(k)
for all
X,Y\in\mathbf{Sm}/k
.

By resolution of singularities,
there exist
\overline{X},\overline{Y}\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/k
such that
\overline{X}-\partial\overline{X}\cong X
,

\overline{Y}-\partial\overline{Y}\cong Y
,
where
\overline{X}
and
\overline{Y}
are proper over
k
.
Proposition
7.7.2
gives equivalences in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k)

\omega^{*}M(X)\simeq M(\overline{X}),\;\omega^{*}M(Y)\simeq M(\overline{Y}),\text{ and }\omega^{*}M(X\times Y)\simeq M(\overline{X}\times\overline{Y}).

Combine these to deduce that (
7.7.7
) is an equivalence.
∎

The right adjoints
\beta^{*}
and
\gamma^{*}
of the functors
\beta_{\sharp}
and
\gamma_{\sharp}

in (
2.8.2
) and (
2.8.5
) form part of the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{SH}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{DA}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DA}(k,\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}(k,\Lambda),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}}

where the functors in the upper row are defined similarly to those in the lower row.

Definition 7.7.5
.

The
logarithmic motivic Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum over
k
with

\Lambda
-coefficients

is defined as

\mathbf{logM\Lambda}:=\beta^{*}\gamma^{*}M(k)\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)

Similarly,

\mathbf{M\Lambda}:=\beta^{*}\gamma^{*}M(k)\in\mathcal{SH}(k)
,
which is equivalent to Voevodsky’s motivic Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum in

[
98
, §6.1]
by
[
37
, Example 3.4, Lemma 4.6]
.

Proposition 7.7.6
.

If
k
is a perfect field that admits resolution of singularities, then there is an equivalence in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)

\mathbf{logM\Lambda}\simeq\omega^{*}\mathbf{M\Lambda}.

Proof.

We have equivalences in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)

\omega^{*}\mathbf{M\Lambda}\simeq\omega^{*}\beta^{*}\gamma^{*}M(k)\simeq\beta^{*}\gamma^{*}\omega^{*}M(k).

Proposition
7.7.2
finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.7.7
.

Suppose
\mathbf{E}
is an oriented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring in
\mathcal{SH}(k)
.
Then
\omega^{*}\mathbf{E}
is an oriented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
.

Proof.

The functor
\omega_{\sharp}\colon\mathcal{SH}(k)\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
is monoidal, so
\omega^{*}
is lax monoidal.
Hence
\omega^{*}
preserves
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings, so
\omega^{*}\mathbf{E}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring.

Since
\mathbf{E}
is oriented,
there exists a morphism
c_{\infty}\colon\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt}\to\Sigma^{2,1}\mathbf{E}
in
\mathcal{SH}(k)

such that the composite morphism

\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt}\to\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt}\to\Sigma^{2,1}\mathbf{E}

is equivalent to
\Sigma^{2,1}\mathbf{1}
,
where
\mathbf{1}\colon\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\operatorname{pt}\to\mathbf{E}
is the unit.
Use
\omega_{\sharp}(\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt})\simeq\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt}
to obtain an orientation
on
\omega^{*}\mathbf{E}
from the orientation on
\mathbf{E}
.
∎

Proposition 7.7.8
.

The spectrum
\mathbf{logM\Lambda}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
.

Proof.

Since
\gamma_{\sharp}\beta_{\sharp}
is monoidal,
it follows that
\beta^{*}\gamma^{*}
is lax monoidal and hence preserves
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings.
Moreover,
the same holds for
\beta^{*}\gamma^{*}\gamma_{\sharp}\beta_{\sharp}
.
To conclude,
observe that
\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}\operatorname{pt}\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring.
∎

Proposition 7.7.9
.

If
k
is a perfect field that admits resolution of singularities,
then
\mathbf{logM\Lambda}
is an oriented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathbf{M\Lambda}
is an oriented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring in
\mathcal{SH}(k)
.
This is a consequence of
[
66
, Theorem 4.1]
and
[
101
]
.
By Proposition
7.7.7
,
\omega^{*}\mathbf{M\Lambda}
is an oriented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
.
Proposition
7.7.6
finishes the proof.
∎

8.
Logarithmic topological Hochschild homology

The objective of this section is to construct the logarithmic motivic spectrum

\mathbf{logTHH}\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)

for all
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
,
and show that the infinite
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
-loops spectrum
\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logTHH}(X)\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
recovers
for all
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
the logarithmic topological Hochschild homology
\mathrm{logTHH}(X)
,
see (
8.5.10
).
If
Y\in\mathbf{Sch}
,
then
\mathrm{logTHH}(Y)
is equivalent to the topological Hochschild homology
\mathrm{THH}(Y)
by Proposition
8.3.2
,
i.e.,

\mathrm{logTHH}
extends
\mathrm{THH}
to the logarithmic setting. The idea of logarithmic topological Hochschild homology is due to Rognes
[
88
]
: here we revisit his construction and show that it gives rise to a motivic invariant, as explained above.

Remark 8.0.1
.

It is a well known fact that topological Hochschild homology is non-
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant,
see e.g., Remark
8.4.2
or
[
38
]
.
In fact, it is
very far
from being
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant,
since
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{THH}\simeq L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{HH}\simeq 0
.
It is therefore impossible to represent
\mathrm{THH}
in
\mathcal{SH}(S)
;
that is,
there is no motivic spectrum
\mathbf{THH}\in\mathcal{SH}(S)
such that

(8.0.1)

\Omega_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}^{\infty}\mathbf{THH}(X)\simeq\mathrm{THH}(X)

for any
X\in\mathbf{Sm}/S
.
Hence, the representability of
\mathrm{logTHH}
in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
shows an important difference between
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
and
\mathcal{SH}(S)
.

Remark 8.0.2
.

The cyclic structure on
\mathbf{logTHH}
can be used to define the refined invariants of
logarithmic negative cyclic homology, logarithmic periodic homology, and logarithmic cyclic homology
\mathbf{logTC}
.
This allows us to construct a logarithmic analogue of the
Bökstedt-Hsiang-Madsen cyclotomic trace map
from
K
-theory to topological cyclic homology
[
26
]
.

Remark 8.0.3
.

For notational clarity,
we shall often add the prefix “
\mathrm{log}
” to cohomology theories extended from schemes to log schemes,
e.g.,
as for
\operatorname{logPic}
,
\mathrm{logK}
, and
\mathrm{logTHH}
. This also agrees with the convention followed in
[
20
]
.

8.1.
\mathrm{THH}
of rings

We briefly review the theory of topological Hochschild homology and topological cyclic homology in the

\infty
-categorical framework developed by Nikolaus and Scholze in
[
76
]
.

Definition 8.1.1
.

Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is an
\infty
-category and
G
is a group with classifying space
\mathrm{B}G
.
An object of the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathcal{C})
is called a
G
-equivariant object of
\mathcal{C}
.
A map in
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathcal{C})
is called a
G
-equivariant map in
\mathcal{C}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
admits small limits.
There is a limit preserving functor of
\infty
-categories

(-)^{hG}\colon\operatorname{Fun}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}

sending
F\colon\mathrm{B}G\to\mathcal{C}
to
\mathop{\rm lim}F
.
This is called the
homotopy fixed point functor
.
The definition of limits gives a naturally induced map

(8.1.1)

F^{hG}=\operatorname{lim}F\to F(*),

where
*
is a unique object of
\mathrm{B}G
.

If
G
is a finite group and
\mathcal{C}
is a stable
\infty
-category, there is a functor

(-)^{tG}\colon\operatorname{Fun}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}.

This is called the
Tate construction
.
We refer to
[
76
, Definition I.1.13]
for more details.
There is a natural transformation
(-)^{hG}\to(-)^{tG}
,
see
[
76
, Theorem I.3.1]
. Following
[
76
, 1.4]
, this can be generalized to the case where
G=\mathbb{T}
is the circle group (as in
[
76
]
, we keep the notation
S^{1}
and
\mathbb{T}
separate to stress on the group structure).

For any integer
n\neq 0
,
we let
C_{n}
denote the cyclic group of order
\lvert n\rvert
.

Definition 8.1.2
.

A
cyclotomic spectrum
is a spectrum
X
with a

\mathbb{T}
-action and

\mathbb{T}
-equivariant maps
X\to X^{tC_{p}}
for every prime
p
.

Remark 8.1.3
.

Nikolaus and Scholze constructed
the
\infty
-category of cyclotomic spectra

\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}

in
[
76
, Definition II.1.6]
.
It underlies a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}^{\otimes}
,
see
[
76
, Construction IV.2.1]
for the details.

Definition 8.1.4
.

Let
A
be an
{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}
-algebra in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
.
The
topological Hochschild homology
of
A
,
denoted
\mathrm{THH}(A)
, is the colimit of the constant diagram

S^{1}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}

with value
A
.
There exists a cyclotomic structure on
\mathrm{THH}(A)
exhibited in

[
76
, §IV.2]
.
Henceforth, we consider
\mathrm{THH}(A)
as an object of
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
.

Remark 8.1.5
.

According to
[
76
, Proposition IV.4.14]
, there is an adjunction

(-)^{triv}:\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}:\mathrm{TC}

with
\mathrm{TC}(X):=\operatorname{map}_{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}},X)

[
76
, Definition II.1.8(i)]
.
As noted in
[
76
, Example II.1.2(ii)]
,
there is an equivalence of cyclotomic spectra
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}\simeq\mathrm{THH}(\mathbb{S})
.
By
[
76
, Theorem I.4.1, Construction IV.2.1]
,
there is a lax symmetric monoidal natural transformation of lax symmetric monoidal functors
\mathrm{can}\colon(-)^{h\mathbb{T}}\to(-)^{t\mathbb{T}}
,
and the functor
\mathrm{TC}
is lax symmetric monoidal.

Definition 8.1.6
.

Let
A
be a ring or an
{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}
-algebra in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
.
Following Nikolaus and Scholze
[
76
]
,
the
negative topological cyclic homology of
A
,
the
topological periodic homology of
A
,
and the
topological cyclic homology of
A

are defined as

(8.1.2)

\mathrm{TC}^{-}(A):=\mathrm{THH}(A)^{h\mathbb{T}},\text{ }\mathrm{TP}(A):=\mathrm{THH}(A)^{t\mathbb{T}},\text{ }\mathrm{TC}(A):=\mathrm{TC}(\mathrm{THH}(A)).

There are canonical maps

\mathrm{TC}(A)\to\mathrm{TC}^{-}(A)\to\mathrm{THH}(A)\text{ and }\mathrm{TC}^{-}(A)\to\mathrm{TP}(A).

Here the first map is obtained by the functor from
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
to the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant spectra,
the second map is obtained from (
8.1.1
),
and the third map is obtained from
\mathrm{can}\colon(-)^{h\mathbb{T}}\to(-)^{t\mathbb{T}}
.

By
[
14
, Corollary 3.4]
,

\mathrm{THH}
,
\mathrm{TC}^{-}
, and
\mathrm{TP}
are fpqc sheaves of spectra on the category of rings,
which implies that
\mathrm{TC}
is an fpqc sheaf too.
By descent,
we obtain

\mathrm{THH},\mathrm{TC}^{-},\mathrm{TP},\mathrm{TC}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{fpqc}(\mathbf{Sch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})

8.2.
Cyclic bar and replete bar constructions

We revisit the cyclotomic structure on logarithmic topological Hochschild homology as discussed in
[
20
]
.

Definition 8.2.1
.

Let
M
be a (discrete) commutative monoid.
The
cyclic bar construction
of
M
,
denoted
\operatorname{B^{cy}}M
,
is the colimit of the constant diagram

S^{1}\to\mathcal{S}

with value
M
.
The base point inclusion
*\to S^{1}
and the collapse map
S^{1}\to*
naturally induce
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant maps

(8.2.1)

M\xrightarrow{\eta}\operatorname{B^{cy}}M\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}M,

where
\mathbb{T}
acts trivially on
M
.
Since
\operatorname{B^{cy}}M
is defined as a colimit,

\eta
is initial among all maps from
M
to a commutative monoid in
\mathcal{S}
with an
\mathbb{T}
-action. See
[
76
, Proposition B.5]
.

Remark 8.2.2
.

An explicit description of
M
as a simplicial set is given as follows.
In simplicial degree
q
,
\operatorname{B^{cy}}M
is given by
M^{\times(q+1)}
.
The face maps are

d_{i}(f_{0},\ldots,f_{q})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(f_{0},\ldots,f_{i-1},f_{i}f_{i+1},f_{i+2},\ldots,f_{q})&\text{if }0\leq i\leq q-1,\\
(f_{q}f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{q-1})&\text{if }i=q,\end{array}\right.

and the degeneracy maps are

s_{i}(f_{0},\ldots,f_{q})=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{i},0,f_{i+1},\ldots,f_{q}).

In
[
103
, §2.3]
,
the cyclic bar construction is denoted by
\mathrm{N}^{cy}
instead of
\operatorname{B^{cy}}
.

Observe that
\eta
is simply the inclusion of zero simplices,
and
\varepsilon
maps
(f_{0},\ldots,f_{q})
to
f_{0}+\cdots+f_{q}
in simplicial degree
q
.
There is a canonical decomposition

(8.2.2)

\operatorname{B^{cy}}M\simeq\coprod_{j\in M}\operatorname{B^{cy}}(M;j),

where
\operatorname{B^{cy}}(M;j):=\varepsilon^{-1}(j)
.

Definition 8.2.3
.

The
replete bar construction
of a monoid
M
is

(8.2.3)

\operatorname{B^{rep}}M:=\operatorname{B^{cy}}M^{{\mathrm{gp}}}\times_{M^{{\mathrm{gp}}}}M.

Rognes discussed this notion in
[
88
, Definition 3.16]
,
and used it as a key input to define logarithmic topological Hochschild homology of pre-log rings.

The maps (
8.2.1
) for
M^{{\mathrm{gp}}}
restricts to
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant maps

(8.2.4)

M\xrightarrow{\eta}\operatorname{B^{rep}}M\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}M.

Moreover,
there is a canonical decomposition

(8.2.5)

\operatorname{B^{rep}}M\simeq\coprod_{j\in M}\operatorname{B^{rep}}(M;j),

where
\operatorname{B^{rep}}(M;j):=\varepsilon^{-1}(j)
.

We will use the next computation to show
(\mathbb{P}^{\bullet},\mathbb{P}^{\bullet-1})
-invariance for logarithmic
topological Hochschild homology.

Proposition 8.2.4
.

There are
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant equivalences

\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{N},j)\simeq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}*&\text{if }j=0\\
\mathbb{T}/C_{j}&\text{if }j>1\end{array}\right.

and

\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{Z},j)\simeq\mathbb{T}/C_{j}\;(j\in\mathbb{Z}),\;\operatorname{B^{rep}}(\mathbb{N},j)\simeq\mathbb{T}/C_{j}\;(j\geq 0).

The naturally induced maps

\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{N},j)\to\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{Z},j)\;(j\geq 1)\text{ and }\operatorname{B^{rep}}(\mathbb{N},j)\to\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{Z},j)\;(j\geq 0)

are
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant equivalences.

Proof.

We refer to
[
88
, Propositions 3.20, 3.21]
.
∎

Definition 8.2.5
.

Suppose
\theta\colon M\to P
is a homomorphism of monoids.
Let
\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P,M)
be the monoid space defined by the
pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{cy}}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{rep}}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{cy}}P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P,M)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{cy}}\theta}

of commutative monoid spaces.

Lemma 8.2.6
.

For all monoids
M
and
N
, there is a canonical
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant equivalence

(8.2.6)

\operatorname{B^{cy}}(M\times N)\cong\operatorname{B^{cy}}M\times\operatorname{B^{cy}}N.

Proof.

The two projections
M\times N\to M,N
naturally induces (
8.2.6
).
In simplicial degree
q
, (
8.2.6
) is given by the shuffle map

(M\times N)^{\times(q+1)}\xrightarrow{\simeq}M^{\times(q+1)}\times N^{\times(q+1)}.

This shows that (
8.2.6
) is an equivalence.
∎

Lemma 8.2.7
.

For all monoids
M
and
N
, there is a canonical
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant equivalence

\operatorname{B^{rep}}(M\times N)\cong\operatorname{B^{rep}}M\times\operatorname{B^{rep}}N.

Proof.

From (
8.2.3
), we have cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{rep}}(M\times N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{cy}}(M\times N)^{{\mathrm{gp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\times N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M\times N)^{{\mathrm{gp}}},}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{rep}}M\times\operatorname{B^{rep}}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{B^{cy}}M^{{\mathrm{gp}}}\times\operatorname{B^{cy}}N^{{\mathrm{gp}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M\times N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{{\mathrm{gp}}}\times N^{{\mathrm{gp}}}.}

Apply Lemma
8.2.6
and compare these two diagrams.
∎

Lemma 8.2.8
.

For
Q:=\mathbb{N}^{r}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{s}
,
P:=\mathbb{N}^{r}\oplus\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{s-1}
, and
M:=0\oplus\mathbb{N}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{s-1}
,
there is a canonical
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant equivalence

\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P,M)\simeq\operatorname{B^{cy}}Q\times_{Q}P.

Proof.

Lemmas
8.2.6
and
8.2.7
yield the
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant equivalences

\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P,M)\simeq(\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{N})^{r}\times\operatorname{B^{rep}}\mathbb{N}\times(\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{Z})^{s-1}

and

\operatorname{B^{cy}}(Q)\simeq(\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{N})^{r}\times\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{Z}\times(\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{Z})^{s-1}.

Using the definition
\operatorname{B^{rep}}\mathbb{N}=\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{Z}\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{N}
, we deduce the desired equivalence.
∎

8.3.
\mathrm{logTHH}
of log schemes

For a pre-log ring
(A,M)
,
Rognes defined the logarithmic topological Hochschild homology of
(A,M)
in
[
88
, Definition 8.11]

as a symmetric spectrum without a cyclotomic structure.
The cyclotomic structure on the topological Hochschild homology of
(A,M)
was first discussed by
Hesselholt-Madsen
[
44
]
;
we will make use of the
\infty
-categorical formulation in
[
45
]
.

For a simplicial set
X
, we adopt the suggestive notation

\mathbb{S}[X]:=\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}X.

For a monoid
M
,

[
19
, Construction 3.7]
yields a natural morphism

\mathrm{THH}(\mathbb{S}[M])\simeq\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}M]\to\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}M]

of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
.

Definition 8.3.1
.

Let
(A,M)
be a pre-log ring.
The
logarithmic topological Hochschild homology of
(A,M)

is the
{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}
-ring in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
given by

(8.3.1)

\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M):=\mathrm{THH}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}M]}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}M].

The notation
\otimes
means the coproduct in the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
.

See again
[
20
, Construction 3.7, Definition 3.8]
for a more explicit description of the Frobenius maps
\varphi_{p}
on
\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M)
, and
[
20
, Proposition 3.6]
(which is in fact
[
62
, Proposition 5.8]
) for an equivalent defintion in terms of the (derived) self intersection of the log diagonal. This latter point of view was used in
[
19
]
.

Example 8.3.2
.

Suppose
A
is a ring.
Then we have
\operatorname{B^{cy}}A^{*}\simeq\operatorname{B^{rep}}A^{*}
since
A^{*}
is a group.
Hence there is a canonical equivalence of cyclotomic spectra

\mathrm{logTHH}(A,A^{*})\simeq\mathrm{THH}(A).

Thus we may view
\mathrm{logTHH}
as an extension of
\mathrm{THH}
as a cyclotomic spectrum.

For a pre-log ring
(A,M)
given by a homomorphism
\theta\colon M\to A
,
we set
M^{a}:=M\oplus_{\theta^{-1}(A^{*})}A^{*}
(this is the logification of
(A,M)
).

Proposition 8.3.3
.

Let
(A,M)
be a pre-log ring.
Then the naturally induced map of cyclotomic spectra

(8.3.2)

\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M)\to\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M^{a})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Since the forgetful functor
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
is conservative by

[
76
, Corollary II.1.7]
,
it suffices to show that (
8.3.2
) is an equivalence of spectra.
This is proved in
[
89
, Theorem 4.24]
.
∎

Proposition 8.3.4
.

Let
R
be a ring.
The functor
\mathbb{L}_{-/R}
on the opposite category of
R
-algebras preserves filtered colimits.

Proof.

This is a consequence of the construction of
\mathbb{L}_{-/R}
.
More generally,

\mathbb{L}_{-/R}
preserves sifted colimits at the simplicial level,
see
[
14
, Construction 2.1]
and
[
19
, §2.5]
.
∎

Proposition 8.3.5
.

The functor
\mathrm{logTHH}
from the category of pre-log rings to
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
preserves filtered colimits.

Proof.

Observe that the forgetful functors
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
and
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
are conservative and preserve small sifted (and hence filtered) colimits by
[
64
, Corollary 3.2.3.2]
and
[
76
, Corollary II.1.7]
.
Hence it suffices to show that the functor

(A,M)\mapsto\mathrm{THH}(A,M)\simeq(A\otimes_{A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}A}A)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M\times M^{\mathrm{gp}}]

to
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
preserve filtered colimits.
This can be checked componentwise.
∎

Recall from
[
60
, Definition 2.44]
that a homomorphism of pre-log rings
(A,M)\to(B,N)
is
a homologically log flat covering
if
A\to B
is faithfully flat and the derived pushout
N\oplus_{M}^{\mathbb{L}}M^{\prime}
is discrete for every homomorphism of monoids
M\to M^{\prime}
.
Using this,
we can define a homologically log flat topology on the opposite category of pre-log rings.

Proposition 8.3.6
.

The presheaf
\mathrm{logTHH}
on the opposite category of pre-log rings is a homologically log flat sheaf of cyclotomic spectra.

Proof.

By
[
76
, Proposition II.1.5(v)]
,
it suffices to show that
\mathrm{logTHH}(-)
and
\mathrm{logTHH}(-)^{tC_{p}}
are sheaves of spectra.
The first one is a sheaf by
[
20
, Proposition 3.10]
.
For the second one,
it suffices to show that
(A,M)\mapsto\wedge^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\tau_{\leq n}\mathbb{Z}_{hC_{p}}
is a sheaf for every integer
n
as in the proof of
[
14
, Corollary 3.4]
.
This follows from
[
20
, Theorem 2.3]
and the fact that
\tau_{\leq n}\mathbb{Z}_{hC_{p}}
is a perfect
\mathbb{Z}
-complex.
∎

Definition 8.3.7
.

For a log scheme
X
,
consider the presheaf of cyclotomic spectra

\mathrm{logTHH}|_{X}(U):=\mathrm{logTHH}(\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U}),\Gamma(U,\mathcal{M}_{U}))

on the category of schemes over
X
.
The
topological Hochschild homology of
X
is defined to be

\mathrm{logTHH}(X):=R\Gamma_{Zar}(X,L_{Zar}\mathrm{logTHH}|_{X}),

where
L_{Zar}
denotes the Zariski sheafification functor.
The
logarithmic negative topological cyclic homology of
X
,
the
logarithmic topological periodic homology of
X
,
and the
logarithmic topological cyclic homology of
X

are defined as

(8.3.3)

\mathrm{logTC}^{-}(X):=\mathrm{logTHH}(X)^{h\mathbb{T}},\text{ }\mathrm{logTP}(X):=\mathrm{logTHH}(X)^{t\mathbb{T}},\text{ }\mathrm{logTC}(A):=\mathrm{TC}(\mathrm{logTHH}(A)).

We note that the logarithmic version
\mathrm{logTHH}(X)
takes into account the log structure on
X
,
while the functors
(-)^{t\mathbb{T}},\mathrm{TC}\colon\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
are as in §
8.1
.

If
X
is a log scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}(R)
for some ring
R
,
consider the Zariski sheaf
\mathbb{L}\Omega^{n}_{X/R}
of complexes of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules on
X
associated with the presheaf on
X
given by

U\mapsto\wedge_{\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U})}^{i}\mathbb{L}_{(\Gamma(U,\mathcal{O}_{U}),\Gamma(U,\mathcal{M}_{U}))/R}.

Proposition 8.3.8
.

Let
(A,M)
be a pre-log ring.
Then the naturally induced map of cyclotomic spectra

(8.3.4)

\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M)\to\mathrm{logTHH}(\operatorname{Spec}(A,M))

is an equivalence.
If
R\to A
is a homomorphism of rings,
then the naturally induced map of complexes

(8.3.5)

\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/R}\to\Gamma_{Zar}(\operatorname{Spec}(A,M),\mathbb{L}\Omega_{\operatorname{Spec}(A,M)/R}^{n})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let us first show that (
8.3.4
) is an equivalence.
Consider the presheaf of cyclotomic spectra

\mathrm{logTHH}|_{(A,M)}(B):=\mathrm{logTHH}(B,M)

on the opposite category of
A
-algebras.
We have the naturally induced map of presheaves of cyclotomic spectra on the opposite category of
A
-algebras

(8.3.6)

\mathrm{logTHH}|_{(A,M)}\to\mathrm{logTHH}|_{\operatorname{Spec}(A,M)}.

If we take the global sections after Zariski sheafification,
then we get
\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M)
for the left-hand side by Proposition
8.3.6
and
\mathrm{logTHH}(\operatorname{Spec}(A,M))
for the right-hand side by definition.
Hence it suffices to show that (
8.3.6
) becomes an equivalence of sheaves of cyclotomic spectra after Zariski sheafification.
For this,
it suffices to show that every stalk of (
8.3.6
) is an equivalence.

A stalk of a presheaf is obtained by a filtered colimit.
Using Proposition
8.3.5
,
we reduce to the case when
A
is a local ring.
In this case,
\mathrm{logTHH}(\operatorname{Spec}(A,M))
becomes
\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M^{a})
.
Together with Proposition
8.3.3
,
we deduce that (
8.3.4
) is an equivalence.

To show that (
8.3.5
) is an equivalence,
use
[
19
, Lemma 3.11]
(resp. Proposition
8.3.4
, resp.
[
19
, Theorem 2.3]
) instead of Proposition
8.3.3
(resp. Proposition
8.3.5
, resp. Proposition
8.3.6
).
∎

Definition 8.3.9
.

Let
X
be a log scheme over
R
,
where
R
is a ring.
The
(relative) logarithmic Hochschild homology of
X
is

\mathrm{logHH}(X/R):=\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{THH}(R)}R.

Observe that if
(A,M)
is a pre-log ring, then
\mathrm{logHH}(\operatorname{Spec}(A,M)/R)
is equivalent to
\mathrm{logHH}((A,M)/R)
in
[
19
, Definition 5.3]
.

Proposition 8.3.10
.

Let
X
be a log scheme over
R
,
where
R
is a ring.
Then
\mathrm{logHH}(X/R)
admits a natural complete exhaustive filtration with the graded pieces

\mathrm{gr}^{n}\mathrm{logHH}(X/R)\simeq R\Gamma_{Zar}(X,\mathbb{L}\Omega^{n}_{X/R})[n]

for all integers
n
.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.3.8
,
this follows from
[
19
, Theorem 5.15]
and Zariski descent.
∎

Proposition 8.3.11
.

The presheaf
\mathrm{logTHH}
on the category of log schemes is a strict fpqc sheaf of cyclotomic spectra.

Proof.

Consider the strict fpqc sheaf given by

\mathrm{logTHH}_{sfpqc}(X):=R\Gamma_{sfpqc}(X,L_{sfpqc}\mathrm{logTHH}|_{X}(U))

for log schemes
X
,
where
sfpqc
is a shorthand for the strict fpqc topology.
Argue as in Proposition
8.3.8
to show that the naturally induced map of cyclotomic spectra

\mathrm{logTHH}(A,M)\to\mathrm{logTHH}_{sfpqc}(\operatorname{Spec}(A,M))

is an equivalence for every pre-log ring
(A,M)
.
This means that
\mathrm{logTHH}
and
\mathrm{logTHH}_{sfpqc}
are equivalent for the affine case and hence are equivalent for the general case by Zariski descent.
It follows that
\mathrm{logTHH}
is a strict fpqc sheaf.
∎

Proposition 8.3.12
.

For a ring
R
and integer
n
,
the presheaf
\mathbb{L}\Omega^{n}_{-/R}
on the category of log schemes is a strict fpqc sheaf of chain complexes.

Proof.

Argue as in Proposition
8.3.11
.
∎

Proposition 8.3.13
.

The presheaves
\mathrm{logTHH}_{h\mathbb{T}}
,
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
on the category of log schemes are strict fpqc sheaves of spectra.

Proof.

Argue as in
[
14
, Corollary 3.4]
to deduce that
\mathrm{logTHH}_{h\mathbb{T}}
is a sheaf from Propositions
8.3.10
and
8.3.12
.
Since the functors
(-)^{h\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathrm{TC}
preserve limits,
Proposition
8.3.11
implies that
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
and
\mathrm{logTC}
are sheaves.
Using the cofiber sequence
\Sigma^{1}(-)_{h\mathbb{T}}\to(-)^{h\mathbb{T}}\to(-)^{t\mathbb{T}}
,
we see that
\mathrm{logTP}
is a sheaf too.
∎

The functor
\lambda\colon\mathbf{Sch}\to\mathbf{lSch}
sending
X\in\mathbf{Sch}
to
X
naturally induces an adjunction

(8.3.7)

\lambda_{\sharp}:\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{fpqc}(\mathbf{Sch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})\rightleftarrows\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{sfpqc}(\mathbf{lSch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}):\lambda^{*}.

As observed in Example
8.3.2
, we have equivalences in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{fpqc}(\mathbf{Sch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})

(8.3.8)

\lambda^{*}\mathrm{logTHH}\simeq\mathrm{THH},\text{ }\lambda^{*}\mathrm{logTC}^{-}\simeq\mathrm{TC}^{-},\text{ }\lambda^{*}\mathrm{logTP}\simeq\mathrm{TP},\text{ and }\lambda^{*}\mathrm{logTC}\simeq\mathrm{TC}.

Construction 8.3.14
.

For
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectra
A
and
B
,
we have an equivalence of spectra
\mathrm{THH}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}B)\simeq\mathrm{THH}(A)\otimes\mathrm{THH}(B)
.
Using Propositions
8.2.6
and
8.2.7
,
we see that the functor
\mathrm{logTHH}
from the category of pre-log rings to
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
is lax symmetric monoidal.
It follows that the functor
\mathrm{logTHH}
from the category of log schemes to
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{yc}\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
is lax symmetric monoidal.
Hence we obtain

\mathrm{logTHH}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{lSch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}))

by
[
64
, Example 2.2.6.9]
,
where the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{lSch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
is the Day convolution.
By applying the lax symmetric monoidal functors
(-)^{h\mathbb{T}}
,
(-)^{t\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathrm{TC}
,
we obtain

\mathrm{logTC}^{-},\mathrm{logTP},\mathrm{logTC}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{lSch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})).

8.4.
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariance of
\mathrm{logTHH}

The purpose of this section is to prove that
\mathrm{logTHH}
is
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariant for all integers
n\geq 1
.
As a consequence,
we will represent
\mathrm{logTHH}(X)
,
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}(X)
,
\mathrm{logTP}(X)
,
and
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}(X)
in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}(S)
whenever
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
and
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
.

Proposition 8.4.1
.

Let
R
be a ring, and let
M
be a monoid.
Then there is an equivalence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in cyclotomic spectra

(8.4.1)

\mathrm{THH}(R)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}(M)]\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{THH}(R[M]).

Proof.

The naturally induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{THH}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{THH}(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{THH}(\mathbb{S}[M])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{THH}(R[M])}

constructs (
8.4.1
) as a map between
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in cyclotomic spectra.
Owing to
[
76
, Corollary II.1.7]
we only need to show that (
8.4.1
) is an equivalence of spectra.
If
M
is a group,
then by
[
96
, Proposition 3.2]
there is a canonical equivalence of spectra

(8.4.2)

H(R[M])\simeq HR\wedge\mathbb{S}[M].

Furthermore, by inspection,
the same proof applies to the case when
M
is a monoid.
Apply
[
44
, Theorem 7.1]
to deduce the desired equivalence.
∎

Remark 8.4.2
.

By Proposition
8.4.1
, there is an equivalence of cyclotomic spectra

\mathrm{THH}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})\simeq\mathrm{THH}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{N})]

for
X\in\mathbf{Sch}
.
Together with Proposition
8.2.4
, we deduce that
\mathrm{THH}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
is not equivalent to
\mathrm{THH}(X)
if
\mathrm{THH}(X)
is nontrivial.
In other words,
\mathrm{THH}
is not
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.

For pre-log rings
(R,M,\theta)
and
(R^{\prime},M^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})
,
let
(R,M)\otimes(R^{\prime},M^{\prime})
be the pre-log ring
(R\otimes R^{\prime},M\oplus M^{\prime},\theta^{\prime\prime})
,
where
\theta^{\prime\prime}
maps
(p,p^{\prime})\in M\oplus M^{\prime}
to
p\otimes p^{\prime}
.

Proposition 8.4.3
.

Let
(R,M)
be a pre-log ring, and let
N\to P
be a homomorphism of monoids.
Then there is a canonical equivalence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in cyclotomic spectra

(8.4.3)

\mathrm{logTHH}((R,M)\otimes(\mathbb{Z}[P],N))\simeq\mathrm{logTHH}(R,M)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P,N)].

Proof.

By definition,
we have

\mathrm{logTHH}((R,M)\times(\mathbb{Z}[P],N))=\mathrm{THH}(R[P])\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}(M\oplus N)]}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}(M\oplus N)].

Owing to Propositions
8.2.6
,
8.2.7
, and
8.4.1

we have equivalences of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in cyclotomic spectra

\displaystyle\mathrm{THH}(R[P])\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}(M\oplus N)]}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}(M\oplus N)]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(\mathrm{THH}(R)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}P])\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}M]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}N]}(\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}M]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}N])

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(\mathrm{THH}(R)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}M]}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}M])\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}(\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}P]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}N]}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}N]).

By definition,
the last object is
\mathrm{logTHH}(R,M)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P,N)]
.
∎

Theorem 8.4.4
.

If
X\in\mathbf{lSch}
, then the naturally induced morphism of spectra

\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\to\mathrm{logTHH}(X\times(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1}))

is an equivalence for all
n\geq 1
.
The same holds for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
.

Proof.

Owing to Propositions
8.3.3
and
8.4.3
, we are reduced to consider
X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
.
The fs log scheme
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
admits the Zariski cover
\{U_{0},\ldots,U_{n}\}
,
where

U_{0}:=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[x_{1}/x_{0},\ldots,x_{n}/x_{0}])

and

U_{i}:=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}(x_{0}/x_{i})\to\mathbb{Z}[x_{0}/x_{i},\ldots,x_{n}/x_{i}])

for
1\leq i\leq n
.
For every nonempty subset
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{r}\}\subset\{0,\ldots,n\}
, we set

U_{I}:=U_{i_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap U_{i_{r}}.

Let
\{e_{0},\ldots,e_{n}\}
be the standard basis in
\mathbb{Z}^{n+1}
,
and let
P_{I}
be the submonoid of
\mathbb{Z}^{n+1}
generated by
e_{i}-e_{j}
for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
and
j\in I
.
We set
P_{\emptyset}:=0
and let

F_{I}
be the face of
P_{I}
generated by
e_{0}-e_{j}
for all
j\in I
if
0\in I
.
By convention,

F_{I}=0
if
0\notin I
.
There is an isomorphism of fs log schemes

U_{I}\cong\operatorname{Spec}(F_{I}\to\mathbb{Z}[P_{I}])

if
I\neq\emptyset
,
and we also have

\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})\cong\operatorname{Spec}(F_{\emptyset}\to\mathbb{Z}[P_{\emptyset}]).

Let
C_{n}
be the
(n+1)
-cube in
\mathcal{S}
naturally defined by
\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P_{I},F_{I})
for all
I\subset\{0,\ldots,n\}
,
which naturally induces an
(n+1)
-cube
\mathbb{S}[C_{n}]
in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
.
We need to show that there is an equivalence of spectra

\operatorname{tfib}(\mathbb{S}[C_{n}])\simeq 0,

which implies the claim by Proposition
2.5.8
.
Owing to Proposition
A.0.10
, it suffices to show there is an equivalence of spectra

\operatorname{tcofib}(\mathbb{S}[C_{n}])\simeq 0.

Since
\mathbb{S}[-]
is a left adjoint,
it preserves small colimits.
Hence it suffices to show there is an equivalence of spaces

(8.4.4)

\operatorname{tcofib}(C_{n})\simeq 0.

It is convenient to consider
Q_{J}:=P_{\{0,\ldots,n\}-J}
and
G_{J}:=F_{\{0,\ldots,n\}-J}
for subsets

J\subset\{0,\ldots,n\}
.
We set
Q_{j}:=Q_{\{j\}}
for
j=0,\ldots,n
.
Let
\{f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\}
be the standard basis in
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.
By identifying
e_{j}-e_{0}
with
f_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,n
, we have isomorphisms of monoids

Q_{j}\cong\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}:x_{j}\geq 0\}

for
j=1,\ldots,n
and

Q_{0}\cong\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}:x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n}\leq 0\}.

Furthermore, we have an isomorphism of monoids

Q_{J}\cong\bigcap_{j\in J}Q_{j}.

Suppose
0\notin J
.
We set
M_{j,J}:=\mathbb{N}f_{j}
if
j\in J
and
M_{j,J}:=\mathbb{Z}f_{j}
if
j\notin J
.
Then there is a canonical isomorphism of monoids

Q_{J}\cong M_{1,J}\times\cdots\times M_{n,J}.

Furthermore, we have
G_{J}=0
.
Lemma
8.2.6
gives a canonical equivalence of spaces

(8.4.5)

\operatorname{B^{rep}}(Q_{J},G_{J})\simeq\operatorname{B^{cy}}M_{1,J}\times\cdots\times\operatorname{B^{cy}}M_{n,J}.

On the other hand, suppose
0\in J
.
Let
i
be any element in
\{0,\ldots,n\}-J
.
We set
M_{j,J}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{N}(f_{j}-f_{i})
if
j\in J
and
M_{j,J}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{Z}(f_{j}-f_{i})
if
j\notin J
.
Then there are isomorphisms of monoids

Q_{J}\cong M_{0,J}^{\prime}\times M_{1,J}^{\prime}\times\cdots\times M_{i-1,J}^{\prime}\times M_{i+1,J}^{\prime}\times\cdots\times M_{n,J}^{\prime}

and

Q_{J-\{0\}}\cong\mathbb{Z}\times M_{1,J}^{\prime}\times\cdots\times M_{i-1,J}^{\prime}\times M_{i+1,J}^{\prime}\times\cdots\times M_{n,J}^{\prime}.

Furthermore,
G_{J}
is the face of
Q_{J}
generated by
f_{0}-f_{i}\in M_{0,J}^{\prime}
.
From Lemma
8.2.8
, we have a canonical equivalence of spaces

(8.4.6)

\operatorname{B^{rep}}(Q_{J},G_{J})\simeq\operatorname{B^{rep}}(Q_{J-\{0\}},G_{J-\{0\}})\times_{Q_{J-\{0\}}}Q_{J}.

Together with
Q_{J}\cong Q_{J-\{0\}}\times_{\mathbb{Z}^{n}}Q_{0}
, we have a canonical equivalence of spaces

(8.4.7)

\operatorname{B^{rep}}(Q_{J},G_{J})\simeq\operatorname{B^{rep}}(Q_{J-\{0\}},G_{J-\{0\}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}^{n}}Q_{0}.

Let
V
be the cofiber of
\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{N}\to\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{Z}
.
Owing to Proposition
8.2.4
, there is a decomposition

V\simeq\coprod_{j\leq 0}V_{j}

with
V_{0}:=\operatorname{cofib}(*\to S_{1}/C_{0})
and
V_{j}:=S_{1}/C_{j}
for
j<0
.
In particular, the canonical map
V\to\mathbb{Z}
factors through
-\mathbb{N}\subset\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
D_{n}
(resp.
D_{n}^{\prime}
) be the
n
-cube naturally associated with
\operatorname{B^{rep}}(Q_{J},G_{J})
for all
J
with
0\notin J
(resp.
0\in J
).
From (
8.4.5
), we see that
D_{n}
is the
n
-cube formulated in Proposition
A.0.8
associated with the maps

\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{N}f_{i})\to\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{Z}f_{i})

for
i=1,\ldots,n
.
Proposition
A.0.8
gives an equivalence of spaces

\operatorname{tcofib}D_{n}\simeq V\times\cdots\times V\;(n\text{ copies of }V).

Together with (
8.4.7
) we have an equivalence of spaces

\operatorname{tcofib}D_{n}^{\prime}\simeq(V\times\cdots\times V)\times_{\mathbb{Z}^{n}}Q_{0}\;(n\text{ copies of }V).

The map
(V\times\cdots\times V)\to\mathbb{Z}^{n}
factors through
(-\mathbb{N})^{n}
, and we have
(-\mathbb{N})^{n}\subset Q_{0}
.
Hence we have an equivalence of spaces

(8.4.8)

\operatorname{tcofib}D_{n}^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{tcofib}D_{n}.

From (
A.0.6
), we have a cofiber sequence

\operatorname{tcofib}D_{n}\to\operatorname{tcofib}D_{n}^{\prime}\to\operatorname{tcofib}C_{n}.

Together with (
8.4.8
) we deduce
\operatorname{tcofib}C_{n}\simeq 0
.

Finally, we use (
8.1.2
) to deduce the claims for
\mathrm{logTC}
and
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
.
∎

Remark 8.4.5
.

To illustrate the previous proof in the case
n=1
,
we note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P_{\emptyset},F_{\emptyset})\simeq*,\;\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P_{\{0\}},F_{\{0\}})\simeq\operatorname{B^{rep}}(-\mathbb{N})\simeq\coprod_{j\geq 0}S^{1}/C_{j},

\displaystyle\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P_{\{1\}},F_{\{1\}})\simeq\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{N})\simeq*\amalg\coprod_{j<0}S^{1}/C_{j},

\displaystyle\operatorname{B^{rep}}(P_{\{0,1\}},F_{\{0,1\}})\simeq\operatorname{B^{cy}}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\coprod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}S^{1}/C_{j}.

The equivalence (
8.4.4
) follows from the cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{j\geq 0}S^{1}/C_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*\amalg\coprod_{j<0}S^{1}/C_{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}S^{1}/C_{j}.}

Construction 8.4.6
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
.
As a consequence of Remark
8.3.14
and Theorem
8.4.4
, we obtain

(8.4.9)

\mathrm{logTHH},\mathrm{logTC}^{-},\mathrm{logTP},\mathrm{logTC}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}^{\rm eff}(S))

by using the
(\mathbb{P}^{\bullet},\mathbb{P}^{\bullet-1})
-localized model in §
3.5
.
Moreover, there is an equivalence of spectra

(8.4.10)

\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)}(\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}X,\mathrm{logTHH})

for every
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
.
The same holds for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
.

Remark 8.4.7
.

We do not know whether (
8.4.10
) holds for every
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
.

8.5.
A
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formula for
\mathrm{logTHH}

Blumberg and Mandell established projective bundle formulas for
\mathrm{THH}
and
\mathrm{TC}
of schemes in
[
25
, Theorem 1.5]
.
They also provided a simpler proof of the loops
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formulas for
\mathrm{THH}
and
\mathrm{TC}
in
[
25
, pp. 1102-1103]
.
Dundas proved the loops
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formula for rings in
[
36
, Proposition 3.3]
.
In this subsection, we will prove the loops
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formula for
\mathrm{logTHH}
.
This will give the loops
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formulas for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
.

Theorem 8.5.1
.

For every
X\in\mathbf{lSch}
, there is an equivalence of spectra

(8.5.1)

\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\simeq\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{logTHH}(X).

The same holds for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
.

Proof.

Let us construct a cocartesian square

(8.5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{N}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}(-\mathbb{N})]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}[\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{Z}].}

Using the descriptions of
\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{N}
and
\operatorname{B^{cy}}\mathbb{Z}
in Proposition
8.2.4
,
we may cancel the terms
S^{1}/C_{j}
for all
j\neq 0
.
Hence our claim follows from the cocartesian square

(8.5.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}\oplus\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{id},0)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\operatorname{id},0)}

Proposition
8.4.3
yields a cocartesian square of cyclotomic spectra

(8.5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTHH}(R,M)\oplus\mathrm{logTHH}(R,M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTHH}((R,M)\otimes\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTHH}((R,M)\otimes\mathbb{Z}[-\mathbb{N}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTHH}((R,M)\otimes\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]).}

for every pre-log ring
(R,M)
.
Thus there is a canonical equivalence of spectra

(8.5.5)

\mathrm{logTHH}(R,M)\simeq\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{logTHH}(R,M).

Applying (
8.1.2
) to (
8.5.4
) yields similar equivalences for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
.

Since (
8.5.5
) is canonical, we can globalize it to obtain (
8.5.1
).
∎

Remark 8.5.2
.

From (
8.5.4
), we deduce the map

\partial\colon\Omega_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\mathrm{logTHH}\to\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathrm{logTHH}.

It is induced by the nontrivial map
d\colon\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}\oplus\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}\to\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{triv}}

obtained from the upper left and lower right corners in (
8.5.3
).
Since
d
admits a section,
\partial
admits a section

\tau\colon\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathrm{logTHH}\to\Omega_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\mathrm{logTHH}.

When restricting to schemes, the former coincides with the map

(8.5.6)

\tau\colon\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathrm{THH}\to\Omega_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\mathrm{THH}

constructed by Blumberg and Mandell
[
25
, pp. 1102-1103]
.

As a consequence of Theorem
8.5.1
, we have an equivalence in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{sNis}(\mathbf{lSch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})

(8.5.7)

\mathrm{logTHH}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\Omega_{T}\mathrm{logTHH}.

By adjunction, we obtain the map

(8.5.8)

T\wedge\mathrm{logTHH}\to\mathrm{logTHH}.

There are similar maps for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
too.

Definition 8.5.3
.

We denote the logarithmic motivic Bott spectrum associated with
\mathrm{logTHH}
by

\mathbf{logTHH}:=\mathrm{Bott}(\mathrm{logTHH})=(\mathrm{logTHH},\mathrm{logTHH},\ldots)\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}_{T}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{sNis}(\mathbf{lSch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})).

Here we use Construction
6.5.2
and the bonding maps
T\wedge\mathrm{logTHH}\to\mathrm{logTHH}
are given by (
8.5.8
).

Similarly,
we define

\mathbf{logTC}^{-}:=\mathrm{Bott}(\mathrm{logTC}^{-}),\text{ }\mathbf{logTP}:=\mathrm{Bott}(\mathrm{logTP}),\text{ and }\mathbf{logTC}:=\mathrm{Bott}(\mathrm{logTC}).

If
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
,
then due to (
8.4.9
),
we obtain

(8.5.9)

\mathbf{logTHH},\mathbf{logTC}^{-},\mathbf{logTP},\mathbf{logTC}\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S).

According to Construction
6.5.3
,
these are homotopy commutative monoids in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
.
Moreover,
there is an equivalence of spectra

(8.5.10)

\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(S)}(\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}X_{+},\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logTHH})

for all
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
due to (
6.5.2
).
By adjunction,
we have an equivalence of spectra

(8.5.11)

\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\simeq\operatorname{map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)}(\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}X_{+},\mathbf{logTHH}).

By construction, we have an equivalence in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)

(8.5.12)

\mathbf{logTHH}\simeq\Sigma^{2,1}\mathbf{logTHH}.

Similar results hold for
\mathbf{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathbf{logTP}
, and
\mathbf{logTC}
too.

Remark 8.5.4
.

We do not know whether (
8.5.10
) or (
8.5.11
) holds for every
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
.

8.6.
The logarithmic cyclotomic trace

In the article
[
26
]
,
Bökstedt, Hsiang, and Madsen constructed the
cyclotomic trace

\mathrm{Tr}(R)\colon\mathrm{K}(R)\to\mathrm{TC}(R)

for every ring
R
.
Since the functor
\mathrm{K}
(resp.
\mathrm{TC}
) satisfies Nisnevich (resp. étale) descent by

[
95
, Theorem 10.8]
(resp.
[
42
, Corollary 3.3.3]
),
the cyclotomic trace can be extended to schemes.
In other words, we have a map

(8.6.1)

\mathrm{Tr}\colon\mathrm{K}\to\mathrm{TC}

in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{Nis}(\mathbf{Sch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
.
The purpose of this subsection is to extend the cyclotomic trace to
\mathbf{lSm}/k
,
where
k
is a perfect field admitting resolution of singularities.
We will also lift (
8.6.1
) to a map of log
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-spectra.

If
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
,
we have the adjoint functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{Sm}/S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mathrm{sh}(\mathbf{cSm}/S)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\simeq\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}}

given by
\lambda_{\sharp}X=X
for
X\in\mathbf{Sm}/S
and
\rho_{\sharp}X=X-\partial X
for
X\in\mathbf{cSm}/S
.

Theorem 8.6.1
.

Suppose
S
is a regular scheme.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
(\square\cup\mathbf{Adm})
-invariant strict Nisnevich sheaf of pointed spaces on
\mathbf{cSm}/S
.
Then for every morphism
f\colon\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}\to\lambda^{*}\mathcal{F}
, there exists a naturally induced morphism

g\colon\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}\to\mathcal{F}

such that
\lambda^{*}g\simeq f
.

Proof.

Let
f^{\prime}
be the composite of the canonical map
\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\to\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}
with
f
.
By adjunction,
f^{\prime}
naturally induces a map
h\colon\lambda_{\sharp}\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\to\mathcal{F}
.
We set
g^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Sing}^{\square\cup\mathbf{Adm}}(h)
and
g:=\mathbb{L}_{sNis}g^{\prime}
.
Owing to Proposition
6.4.2
,
we have
\mathbb{L}_{sNis}f^{\prime}\simeq f
.
To show
\lambda^{*}g\simeq f
,
it suffices to show
\lambda^{*}g^{\prime}\simeq f^{\prime}
since

\mathbb{L}_{sNis}\lambda^{*}\simeq\lambda^{*}\mathbb{L}_{Nis}
.

There is a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\operatorname{Sing}^{\square\cup\mathbf{Adm}}\lambda_{\sharp}\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}\operatorname{Sing}^{\square\cup\mathbf{Adm}}(\mathcal{F}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{*}g^{\prime}}

where
\alpha
is naturally induced by the canonical map

\lambda_{\sharp}\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\to\operatorname{Sing}^{\square\cup\mathbf{Adm}}\lambda_{\sharp}\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}}).

Since
\mathcal{F}
is
(\square\cup\mathbf{Adm})
-invariant,
\beta
is an equivalence of sheaves.
Therefore, it suffices to show that
\alpha
is an equivalence of presheaves.

For
X\in\mathbf{Sm}/k
the
q
-simplex of the simplicial model of

\lambda^{*}\operatorname{Sing}^{\square\cup\mathbf{Adm}}\lambda_{\sharp}\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})(X)

is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sing}_{q}^{\square\cup\mathbf{Adm}}\lambda_{\sharp}\operatorname{Sing}_{q}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})(X)

\displaystyle\cong\mathop{\rm colim}_{Y\in\mathbf{Adm}\downarrow X\times\square^{q}\times\mathbb{A}^{q}}\operatorname{Hom}(Y,\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{Hom}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{2q},\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}}).

Here, the second isomorphism follows from (
6.3.1
).
Therefore, there is an equivalence

\lambda^{*}\operatorname{Sing}^{\square\cup\mathbf{Adm}}\lambda_{\sharp}\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{Sing}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}}).

It follows that
\alpha
is an equivalence of presheaves of spaces.
∎

Corollary 8.6.2
.

Assume that
k
is a perfect field that admits resolution of singularities.
Then there exists a map

\mathrm{logTr}\colon\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}\to\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC}

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(k)
such that for every
X\in\mathbf{Sm}/k
, the map

\mathrm{logTr}(X)\colon\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}(X)\to\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC}(X)

is equivalent to the cyclotomic trace

\mathrm{Tr}(X)\colon\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}(X)\to\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{TC}(X)
.

Proof.

Recall the equivalence

\psi_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{SmAdm}}(k)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{Adm}}(k)

in Remark
4.0.14
.
To conclude, we apply Theorem
8.6.1
to
\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}\to\lambda^{*}\psi_{\sharp}\mathrm{logTC}
.
∎

Proposition 8.6.3
.

Assume that
k
is a perfect field that admits resolution of singularities.
Then
\mathrm{logTr}
is a homomorphism of homotopy commutative monoids.

Proof.

Due to Theorem
6.4.4
, it suffices to show the naturally induced diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\wedge(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC}\wedge\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC}}

commutes in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{Adm}}(k)
.
By
[
24
, Theorem 1.12]

the cyclotomic trace
\mathrm{K}\to\mathrm{TC}
is a homomorphism of homotopy commutative monoids.
To conclude,
we appeal to the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\wedge(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}\wedge\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}.}

∎

Proposition 8.6.4
.

There is a commutative square in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{Zar}(\mathbf{Sch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})

(8.6.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{TC}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{TC}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}

Here the vertical maps are given by the loops
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formulas for
\mathrm{K}
and
\mathrm{TC}
.

Proof.

Blumberg and Mandell showed in
[
25
, p. 1102]
that the square

(8.6.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathrm{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathrm{TC}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathrm{TC}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}

commutes, where the left vertical map is the map appearing in the construction of Bass
K
-theory,
and the right vertical map is (
8.5.6
).

Suppose
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{Zar}(\mathbf{Sch},\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
and there is a map
\tau\colon\Sigma_{S^{1}}\mathcal{F}\to\Omega_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\mathcal{F}
.
The cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\mathcal{F} }

yields a map
\Omega_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\mathcal{F}\to\Sigma_{S^{1}}\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathcal{F}
.
Compose with
\tau
to get a map
\mathcal{F}\to\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mathcal{F}
.
It is a classical fact that this coincides with the loops
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formula when
\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{K}
.
The same holds when
\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{TC}
by Remark
8.5.2
.
Hence we can obtain (
8.6.2
) from (
8.6.3
).
∎

Theorem 8.6.5
.

Assume that
k
is a perfect field admitting resolution of singularities.
There is a homomorphism

\mathbf{logTr}\colon\mathbf{logKGL}\to\mathbf{logTC}

of homotopy commutative monoids in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
such that

\lambda^{*}\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logTr}\colon\lambda^{*}\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logKGL}\to\lambda^{*}\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logTC}

is equivalent to the cyclotomic trace
\mathrm{Tr}\colon\mathrm{K}\to\mathrm{TC}
in
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{Nis}(\mathbf{Sm}/k,\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
.

Proof.

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{TC}(\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}((\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\wedge\mathbb{P}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{TC}((\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}})\wedge\mathbb{P}^{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}

owing to Proposition
8.6.4
,
where the vertical maps are given by the loops
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle formulas.
This means that the outer diagram of the naturally induced diagram

(8.6.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\times{\mathrm{Gr}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega^{*}\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTr}}

in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}^{\mathbf{Adm}}(k)
commutes.
Together with Theorem
6.4.4
, we deduce that the square
(a)
in (
8.6.4
) commutes.
It follows that we have a map
\mathbf{logTr}
from

\mathbf{logKGL}\simeq(\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K},\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K},\ldots)

to

\mathbf{logTC}\simeq(\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC},\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC},\ldots).

According to (
6.5.2
), the map
