\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logTr}
takes the form

(\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K},\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K},\ldots)\to(\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC},\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTC},\ldots),

with
\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{n}\mathrm{logTr}
in level
n
for every integer
n\geq 0
.
It follows that
\lambda^{*}\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logTr}
is given by

\lambda^{*}\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{n}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTr}\simeq\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{n}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{logTr}
.
Hence we have a natural equivalence

\lambda^{*}\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}}^{\infty}\mathbf{logTr}\simeq\mathrm{Tr}.

To show
\mathbf{logTr}\colon\mathbf{logKGL}\to\mathbf{logTC}
is a homomorphism of homotopy commutative monoids,
it suffices to show that the diagram of
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{\wedge 2}
-spectra

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K}\wedge\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K},\ldots)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\omega^{*}\Omega_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{K},\ldots)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Omega_{S^{1}}\mathrm{logTC}\wedge\Omega_{S^{1}}\mathrm{logTC},\ldots)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Omega_{S^{1}}\mathrm{logTC},\ldots)}

commutes.
Use Proposition
8.6.3
to conclude.
∎

Proposition 8.6.6
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
.
Let
\alpha\colon\mathbf{E}\to\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
be a homomorphism of homotopy commutative monoids in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
.
If
\mathbf{E}
has a multiplicative orientation,
then
f
induces a multiplicative orientation on
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{**}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\prime**}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\prime**}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

commutes.
Hence
\alpha\mathrm{F}_{\mathbf{E}}=\mathrm{F}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\alpha
.
It follows that we have
\mathrm{F}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(x,y)=x+y-\alpha(u)xy
.
To conclude, observe that
\alpha(u)
is invertible since
u
is invertible.
∎

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
is a scheme.
Since
\mathbf{logTHH}
,
\mathbf{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathbf{logTP}
,
and
\mathbf{logTC}
are homotopy commutative monoids in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)
by Construction
6.5.3
,
we can define the graded rings

\mathbf{logTHH}^{**}(X),\text{ }(\mathbf{logTC}^{-})^{**}(X),\text{ },\mathbf{logTP}^{**}(X),\text{ and }\mathbf{logTC}^{**}(X)

for all
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
according to Definition
7.0.1
.

Theorem 8.6.7
.

If
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
is a scheme,
then

\mathbf{logTHH},\mathbf{logTC}^{-},\mathbf{logTP},\mathbf{logTC}\in\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)

are multiplicatively oriented.

Proof.

As a consequence of the projective bundle formula for
K
-theory, we have an isomorphism of rings

\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})\cong\mathrm{K}_{0}(S)[[x]].

Consider the naturally induced commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}_{0}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}_{0}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{logTC}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{2,1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{2,1}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{2,1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where the upper left square comes from Proposition
8.6.4
.
Let
c_{\infty}\in\mathbf{logTC}^{2,1}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt})
and
c_{1}\in\mathbf{logTC}^{2,1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt})
be the images of
x\in\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt})
.
Since the image of the unit
\mathbf{1}\in\mathrm{K}_{0}(X)
in
K_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt})
coincides with the image of
x\in\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}/\operatorname{pt})
in
K_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}/\operatorname{pt})
,
c_{1}
is the image of
\mathbf{1}\in\mathrm{K}_{0}(X)
.
It follows that
c_{\infty}
gives an orientation of
\mathbf{logTC}
.

The homomorphism

\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})\to\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{P}^{\infty})

naturally induced by (
7.5.1
) is isomorphic to the homomorphism

\mathrm{K}_{0}(S)[[x]]\to\mathrm{K}_{0}(S)[[x,y]]

sending
x
to
x+y-xy
.
Consider the periodicity isomorphism
\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}\cong\mathbf{logTC}^{2,1}
,
and let
\gamma
be the image of the unit
1\in\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}
in
\mathbf{logTC}^{2,1}
.
Since
\mathbf{logTC}
is oriented,
the naturally induced commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}(\mathbb{P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{P}^{\infty})}

can be written as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}_{0}(S)[[x]]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{K}_{0}(S)[[x,y]]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}[[x/\gamma]]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{logTC}^{0,0}[[x/\gamma,y/\gamma]].}

The left (resp. right) vertical homomorphism sends
x
to
x/\gamma
(resp.
x
to
x/\gamma
and
y
to
y/\gamma
).
Thus the lower horizontal homomorphism sends
x/\gamma
to
x/\gamma+y/\gamma-xy/\gamma^{2}
.
Setting
\beta=\gamma^{-1}
it follows that the formal group law on
\mathbf{logTC}
is given by
x+y-\beta xy
.

For
\mathbf{logTHH}
,
\mathbf{logTC}^{-}
and
\mathbf{logTP}
, use Proposition
8.6.6
.
∎

Proposition 8.6.8
.

If
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
is a scheme,
there are isomorphisms of graded rings

\mathbf{logTHH}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}}(r))\cong\mathbf{logTHH}^{**}[[c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}]]

and

\mathbf{logTHH}^{**}({\mathrm{Gr}})\cong\mathbf{logTHH}^{**}[[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots]].

Similar results hold for
\mathbf{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathbf{logTP}
, and
\mathbf{logTC}
.

Proof.

Combine Corollary
7.4.9
and Theorem
8.6.7
.
∎

Proposition 8.6.9

(Thom equivalence)
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
,
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
, and
\mathcal{E}\to X
is a rank
d
vector bundle.
Then there exists a canonical equivalence of spectra

\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{logTHH}(\mathrm{Th}(\mathcal{E})).

The same holds for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
.

Proof.

Combine Proposition
7.3.9
, (
8.5.11
), and (
8.5.12
).
∎

Proposition 8.6.10
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{Sch}
and
X\in\mathbf{SmlSm}/S
is of the form
(\underline{X},Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{r})
and
Z
is a strict smooth closed subscheme of
X
strict normal crossing with
Z_{1}+\cdots+Z_{r}
.
Then there exists a cofiber sequence of spectra

\mathrm{logTHH}(Z)\to\mathrm{logTHH}(X)\to\mathrm{logTHH}({\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}X,E),

where
E
is the exceptional divisor on
{\mathrm{Bl}}_{Z}X
.

The same holds for
\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
,
\mathrm{logTP}
, and
\mathrm{logTC}
.

Proof.

Immediate from Theorem
3.2.21
and Proposition
8.6.9
.
∎

8.7.
Representability of Nygaard completed prismatic cohomology

In
[
14
, Theorem 1.12(2)]
,
Bhatt-Morrow-Scholze uses Čech descent for the quasi-syntomic topology to construct their
“motivic filtration”

\mathrm{Fil}^{\ast}\mathrm{TC}^{-}(A;\mathbb{Z}_{p})

on negative topological cyclic homology
\mathrm{TC}^{-}(A;\mathbb{Z}_{p})

— here
A
is any quasi-syntomic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra in the sense of
[
14
, Definition 4.10]
.
The
0
-th graded piece is denoted by

\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A}:=\mathrm{gr}^{0}\mathrm{TC}^{-}(A;\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

The Nygaard filtration
\mathcal{N}^{\geq\ast}\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}
on
\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A}
is constructed
in
[
14
, Theorem 7.2(2)]
.
Bhatt-Scholze’s
[
15
, Theorem 13.1]
shows that
\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A}
is equivalent to the
completion of the prismatic cohomology
\mathbf{\Delta}_{A}
with respect to the Nygaard filtration.

Remark 8.7.1
.

Suppose
A
is a smooth
k
-algebra and
k
is a perfect field of characteristic
p
.
Then, by
[
14
, Theorem 1.10]
,
there is an equivalence between
\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A}
and the crystalline cohomology of
A
.
In particular,
this implies that
\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A}
is not
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant,
and hence there is no (
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant) motivic
S^{1}
-spectrum
\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A}\in\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(A)

providing an equivalence of spectra

\operatorname{map}_{\mathcal{SH}_{S^{1}}(A)}(\Sigma_{S^{1}}^{\infty}\operatorname{Spec}(B)_{+},\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A})\simeq\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{B}

for every smooth
A
-algebra
B
.

Our joint work with Lundemo
[
20
]
shows that logarithmic negative topological cyclic homology

\mathrm{logTC}^{-}
also admits a motivic filtration —
constructed using Čech descent for a log variant of the quasi-syntomic topology.
The corresponding
0
-th graded piece furnishes a log extension
\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{(A,M)}

of
\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{A}
for every log quasi-syntomic integral pre-log ring
(A,M)
.
This can be further extended to log schemes, giving rise to the Nygaard completed log prismatic cohomology
R\Gamma_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}(\mathfrak{X})
.
With a log variant of
[
14
, Proposition 7.8]
,
it is possible to show that
R\Gamma_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}(-)
is

(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariant for every integer
n\geq 1
.

We conclude this section by stating a result from
[
18
]
. The following definition is a straightforward adaptation of our constructions to the setting of formal schemes.

Definition 8.7.2
.

Let
S
be a quasi-compact and quasi-separated
p
-adic formal scheme.
We denote by
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{\rm eff}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be the monoidal stable
\infty
-category of formal log motives over
S
, that is the full
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}_{s{\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{FlSm}/S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
spanned by the objects
M
that are local with respect to the maps
\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\mathfrak{X}\times((\mathbb{P}^{n})^{\wedge}_{p},(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{\wedge}_{p}))\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}(\mathfrak{X})
for all
\mathfrak{X}\in\mathbf{FlSm}/S
and
n\geq 1
. We denote by
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
the category of
T
-spectra in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{\rm eff}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, defined in the obvious way.

Theorem 8.7.3

(
[
18
, Theorem 3.5]
)
.

Let
S
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme. There are oriented ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fil}\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}^{\rm eff}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
such that for all
\mathfrak{X}\in\mathbf{FlSm}/S
we have

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}})\simeq R\Gamma_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r]

and

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}(S,\mathbb{Z}_{p})}(\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathfrak{X}),\Sigma^{r,s}\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fil}\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}})\simeq\mathrm{Fil}_{N}^{\geq s}R\Gamma_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}(\mathfrak{X})\{s\}[r],

where
\{s\}
denotes the Breuil–Kisin twist.
If
S\in\mathrm{QSyn}/R
for
R
perfectoid,
\mathbf{E}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fil}\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}
live in
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}(S,A_{\inf}(R))
, and there are oriented ring spectra
\mathbf{E}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}^{\rm nc}}
and
\mathbf{E}^{\mathrm{Fil}\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}^{\rm nc}}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}(S,A_{\inf}(R)))
, together with equivalences

(8.7.1)

\mathbf{E}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{R},\theta}R\simeq\mathbb{E}^{\rm\widehat{dR}}\qquad\mathbf{E}^{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}^{\rm nc}}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{R},\theta}R\simeq\mathbb{E}^{\rm dR}

of oriented ring spectra in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{log}\mathcal{FDA}(S,R))
, where
\theta\colon A_{\rm inf}(R)\to R
is Fontaine’s period map, and
\mathbb{E}^{\rm\widehat{dR}}
is the motivic spectrum representing (completed) derived de Rham cohomology.

Among the applications of the results constructed in this work, we deduce from the above representabiliy results Gysin sequences, blow-up formulas and we compute the prismatic cohomology of Grassmannians. See
[
18
]
for a full discussion.

9.
Realization functors

Realization functors are very useful for the purpose of comparing
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory with
classical homotopy theories.
For example,
Levine
[
61
]
used the Betti realization to prove that the naturally induced functor

\mathbf{SH}\to\mathbf{SH}(k)

is fully faithful when
k
is an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
.

The Betti realization is not available if the characteristic of
k
is nonzero;
however, the
\ell
-adic étale realization is a useful replacement in positive characteristic.
Zargar
[
105
]
used the
\ell
-adic étale realization to prove the naturally induced functor

\mathbf{SH}[1/p]\to\mathbf{SH}(k)[1/p]

is fully faithful when
k
is an algebraically closed field of exponential characteristic
p
.
A closely related result due to Wilson and Østvær
[
104
]
shows the naturally induced homomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{SH}}(\mathbb{S},\Sigma^{n}\mathbb{S})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{SH}(k)}(\mathbf{1},\Sigma^{n,0}\mathbf{1})

is an isomorphism for every integer
n
;
their proof employs Witt vectors to reduce to algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero
(
\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbf{1}
denotes the topological and motivic sphere, respectively).

Remark 9.0.1
.

In order to construct the
p
-adic étale realization within the framework of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory,
one needs to invert the exponential characteristic
p
because étale cohomology with

\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-coefficient is not
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.
In the setting of logarithmic motives,
Merici
[
67
]
constructed a
p
-adic realization functor

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}_{{l\acute{e}t}}(k)\to\mathcal{D}(W(k))^{\mathrm{op}}

to the opposite derived
\infty
-category of
W(k)
-modules for all field
k
,
by showing that log de Rham-Witt sheaves admit a log transfer structure, and that they are
(\mathbb{P}^{n},\mathbb{P}^{n-1})
-invariant for every
n\geq 1
.

In this section,
we will explain how the log étale realization can be used to somehow incorporate
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-coefficients.
Moreover,
we will also construct enhanced Kato-Nakayama realization functors

\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)\to\mathcal{S}\text{ and }\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}

for every subfield
k
of
\mathbb{C}
and
S\in\mathbf{lSch}/k
,
which is a logarithmic analogue of the Betti realization in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy theory.

9.1.
\square
-invariance of Kummer étale cohomology

In this subsection,
we show that log étale cohomology groups are
\square
-invariant for locally constant sheaves.
This will be used to construct log étale realization functors in §
9.2
.

Definition 9.1.1
.

Recall from
[
72
, Definition 2.5]
that a
log geometric point

of an fs log scheme
X
is a morphism
f\colon Z\to X
of saturated log schemes such that
\underline{Z}
is the
spectrum of a separably closed field and the multiplication homomorphism of abelian groups

\cdot n\colon\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}(Z)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}(Z)
is an isomorphism for every integer
n\geq 2

prime to the characteristic of
k
.
The log geometric points of
X
form enough points for the small Kummer étale site
X_{{k\acute{e}t}}
.

Lemma 9.1.2
.

Let
X
be a log geometric point.
If
h\colon X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to X
denotes the projection, then we have

\mathbb{R}_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}h_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)\simeq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{Z}/n&\text{if }i=2,\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

for all integer
n\geq 2
prime to the characteristic of
X
.

Proof.

By
[
72
, Paragraph 4.7(ii)]
, we reduce to the case when
X
has trivial log structure.
There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

where
f
is the projection,
i
is the inclusion at
\infty
, and
j
is the complement of
i
.
From the localization sequence
[
72
, §2.8(4)]

0\to j_{!}j^{*}\to\operatorname{id}\to i_{*}i^{*}\to 0,

we have a distinguished triangle

\mathbb{R}_{{\acute{e}t}}h_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathbb{R}_{{\acute{e}t}}f_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathbb{Z}/n\to\mathbb{R}_{{\acute{e}t}}h_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)[1].

To conclude,
observe that

[
4
, Corollaire IX.4.7]
gives
\mathbb{R}_{{\acute{e}t}}^{i}f_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)=\mathbb{Z}/n
for
i=0,2
and
\mathbb{R}_{{\acute{e}t}}^{i}f_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)=0
otherwise.
∎

Lemma 9.1.3
.

Let
X
be a log geometric point.
Then we have

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\operatorname{pt}_{\mathbb{N}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\simeq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{Z}/n&\text{if }i=0,1\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

for all integer
n\geq 2
prime to the characteristic of
X
.

Proof.

Suppose
X
has characteristic
p
and set
G:=\overline{\mathcal{M}(X)}^{\mathrm{gp}}
.
For every prime
\ell\neq p
,
the homomorphism
\cdot\ell\colon G\to G
is an isomorphism of abelian groups.
This implies

\pi(G):=\operatorname{Hom}\big(G,\coprod_{\ell\neq p}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}\big)=0.

Hence
\pi(G\oplus\mathbb{Z})\cong\pi(\mathbb{Z})
.
Owing to
[
73
, Proposition 1.2]
, there are isomorphisms of abelian groups

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\operatorname{pt}_{\mathbb{N}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\cong H^{i}(\pi(G\oplus\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/n)\cong H^{i}(\pi(\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}/n)\cong H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\underline{X}\times\operatorname{pt}_{\mathbb{N}},\mathbb{Z}/n),

where the second and third groups are the profinite cohomology groups.
Hence we reduce to the case when
X
has trivial log structure.

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\operatorname{pt}_{\mathbb{N}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

where
f
,
g
, and
h
are the projections,
i
is the inclusion at
\infty
, and
j
is the complement of
i
.
From the localization sequence
[
72
, §2.8(4)]

0\to j_{!}j^{*}\to\operatorname{id}\to i_{*}i^{*}\to 0,

we deduce the distinguished triangle

\mathbb{R}_{k{\acute{e}t}}h_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathbb{R}_{k{\acute{e}t}}f_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathbb{R}_{k{\acute{e}t}}g_{!}\mathbb{Z}/n\to\mathbb{R}_{k{\acute{e}t}}h_{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n)[1].

Hence due to Lemma
9.1.2
, there is an exact sequence

(9.1.1)

\begin{split}0&\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{1}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{1}(X\times\operatorname{pt}_{\mathbb{N}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\\
&\to\mathbb{Z}/n\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{2}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{2}(X\times\operatorname{pt}_{\mathbb{N}},\mathbb{Z}/n).\end{split}

Owing to
[
72
, Proposition 5.3]
, we have the vanishing

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\operatorname{pt}_{\mathbb{N}},\mathbb{Z}/n)=0

for every integer
i\geq 2
.
Apply
[
50
, Theorem 7.4]
to
X\times\square
to deduce

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)\cong H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathbb{Z}/n)=0

for every integer
i\geq 1
.
Together with (
9.1.1
), this finishes the computation.
∎

Lemma 9.1.4
.

Let
X
be a log geometric point.
Then we have

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{Z}/n&\text{if }i=0,\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

for all integers
n\geq 2
.

Proof.

Suppose
X
has characteristic
p
.
It suffices to show the claim when
n
is prime.
If
n=p
,
the Artin-Schreier sequence

0\to\mathbb{Z}/p\to\mathcal{O}\xrightarrow{F-\operatorname{id}}\mathcal{O}\to 0

gives a long exact sequence

(9.1.2)

\begin{split}\cdots\to&H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/p)\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\square,\mathcal{O})\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\square,\mathcal{O})\\
\to&H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i+1}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/p)\to\cdots.\end{split}

Due to
[
56
, Proposition 6.5]
(see also
[
77
, Proposition 3.27]
), there is an isomorphism of abelian groups

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\square,\mathcal{O})\cong H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}).

This group is trivial for
i\geq 1
.
Hence we conclude from (
9.1.2
).

Suppose
n\neq p
.
Due to
[
72
, Proposition 5.3]
, we have the vanishing

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)=0

for every
i\geq 3
.
The exact sequence (
9.1.1
) and Lemma
9.1.3
yield the exact sequence

0\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{1}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathbb{Z}/n\to\mathbb{Z}/n\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{2}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)\to 0.

Hence, to finish the proof, it suffices to show the vanishing

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{1}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)=0.

The Kummer sequence

0\to\mu_{n}\to\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}\xrightarrow{\cdot n}\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}\to 0

induces the exact sequence

H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{0}(X\times\square,\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{gp}}})\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{0}(X\times\square,\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{gp}}})\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{1}(X\times\square,\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{1}(X\times\square,\mathcal{M}^{{\mathrm{gp}}}).

Apply Proposition
5.1.7
to conclude.
∎

Theorem 9.1.5
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
For every locally constant Kummer étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X_{k{\acute{e}t}}
,
the projection
p\colon X\times\square\to X
naturally induces an isomorphism of graded rings

p^{*}\colon H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X,\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\cong}H_{k{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X\times\square,p^{*}\mathcal{F}).

Proof.

There is a Leray spectral sequence

E_{2}^{pq}=H^{q}_{k{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{R}^{p}_{k{\acute{e}t}}p_{*}p^{*}\mathcal{F})\Rightarrow H^{p+q}(X\times\square,p^{*}\mathcal{F}).

Hence it suffices to show the vanishing

\mathbb{R}^{i}_{k{\acute{e}t}}p_{*}p^{*}\mathcal{F}=0

for every integer
i\geq 1
.
Let
x
be a log geometric point of
X
.
We reduce to showing the vanishing

(\mathbb{R}^{i}_{k{\acute{e}t}}p_{*}p^{*}\mathcal{F})_{x}=0.

There is a naturally induced cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

Applying the proper base change theorem
[
72
, Theorem 5.1]

i^{*}\mathbb{R}p_{*}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathbb{R}p^{\prime*}i_{*}^{\prime}

we reduce to the case when
X
is a log geometric point.
In this case,
\mathcal{F}
is a constant sheaf because it is locally constant.
Lemma
9.1.4
finishes the proof.
∎

Thanks to the following,
several results in this section also hold for log étale cohomology.

Proposition 9.1.6
.

Let
X
be an fs log scheme.
Then for every torsion abelian group
A
,
there is a canonical isomorphism of graded rings

H_{{k\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X,A)\cong H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X,A).

Proof.

Follows from
[
73
, Proposition 5.4(2)]
.
∎

9.2.
The log étale realization

Artin and Mazur
[
5
]
defined the étale homotopy type of a scheme
X
as a pro-object
in the homotopy category of simplicial sets.
Friedlander
[
40
]
promoted this to a pro-object of simplicial sets.
More recently,
Lurie established that the étale homotopy type can given by the shape of an
\infty
-topos,
see
[
63
, Example 7.1.6.9]
.
Suppose
k
is a field of characteristic
p
.
For every prime
\ell\neq p
,
Isaksen
[
52
, Theorem 6]
constructed a left Quillen functor,
whose underlying functor of
\infty
-categories is the étale realization functor

\mathcal{H}(k)\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}

sending
X\in\mathbf{Sm}/k
to its étale homotopy type for every prime
\ell\neq p
,
where
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
,
reviewed in Definition
9.2.2
below,
is a certain localization of the
\infty
-category of pro-spaces
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})
.
An equivalent construction in terms of shape theory was given by Hoyois in
[
47
]
.
Based on this,
Zargar
[
105
]
constructed the stable étale realization functor.
To construct the log étale realization functor one may proceed as Isaksen in
[
52
]
;
however,
we will follow the method developed by Hoyois and Zargar using
\infty
-categories.

We refer to
[
63
, Definition 5.2.4.5]
for the definition of accessible functors.

Definition 9.2.1
.

For any presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with finite limits,
let
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{C})
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S})

spanned by accessible functors
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
preserving finite limits.

Definition 9.2.2
.

Let
X=\{X_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
be a pro-space,
A
an abelian group, and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The
i
th singular cohomology of
X
with
A
-coefficients is the abelian group

H^{i}(X,A):=\pi_{i}(\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})}(X,A))\cong\mathop{\rm colim}_{\alpha\in I}H^{i}(X_{\alpha},A).

Let
H\mathbb{Z}/\ell
denote the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum associated with the abelian group
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
.
A map
f\colon Z\to Y
in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})
is an
(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local equivalence

if the naturally induced homomorphism of graded rings

f^{*}\colon H^{*}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\to H^{*}(Z,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)

is an isomorphism.
A pro-space
X\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})
is
(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local
if the map

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})}(Y,X)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})}(Z,X)

is an equivalence of spaces for every
(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local equivalence
f\colon Z\to Y
in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})
.

Let
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})
spanned by the

(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local pro-spaces.

There is an analogous definition for spectra as follows.

Definition 9.2.3
.

A map
f\colon Z\to Y
in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
is an

(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local equivalence

if the naturally induced map of spaces

f^{*}\colon\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})}(Y,H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})}(Z,H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)

is an equivalence.
A pro-spectrum
X\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
is
(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local
if the map of spaces

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})}(Y,X)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})}(Z,X)

is an equivalence of spaces for every
(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local equivalence
f\colon Z\to Y
in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
.

Let
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
denote the
full subcategory of
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})
spanned by the
(H\mathbb{Z}/\ell)^{*}
-local pro-spectra.

Next we recall the definition of shapes,
see
[
63
, §7.1.6]
and
[
105
, §4]
.

Definition 9.2.4
.

A
geometric morphism
of
\infty
-topoi is a functor
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
of
\infty
-topoi that admits
a left exact left adjoint
f^{*}\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
.
The functor
f^{*}
admits a pro-left adjoint

f_{!}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{Y})

satisfying
f_{!}(X)(Y):=\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{X}}(X,f^{*}Y)
.
By
[
63
, Proposition 6.3.4.1]
,
there exists a unique geometric morphism
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
up to equivalence for every
\infty
-topoi
\mathcal{X}
.
Furthermore,
its left adjoint
f^{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{X}
is the constant sheaf functor.

The
shape of
\mathcal{X}
is defined to be

(9.2.1)

\Pi(\mathcal{X}):=f_{!}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}),

where
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{X}}
denotes the final object of
\mathcal{X}
.

Let
\mathcal{T}\mathrm{opos}^{R}
denote the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-topoi, where
1
-morphisms are geometric morphisms.
According to
[
63
, Remark 7.1.6.15]
, there is a functor

(9.2.2)

\Pi\colon\mathcal{T}\mathrm{opos}^{R}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})

sending
\mathcal{X}
to
\Pi(\mathcal{X})
, which preserves small colimits.

Definition 9.2.5
.

For
X\in\mathbf{lSch}
, we set

\Pi^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X):=\Pi(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(X_{{l\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{S})).

For any prime
\ell
, let
\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X)
be its image in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
.

For every abelian group
A
,
(
9.2.1
) gives an isomorphism of abelian groups

\pi_{*}(\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(X_{{l\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{S})}(X,A))\cong\pi_{*}(\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})}(\Pi^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X),A)).

Hence we obtain an isomorphism of abelian groups

(9.2.3)

H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X,A)\cong H^{*}(\Pi^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X),A).

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in
\mathbf{lSch}
.
Then the
functor
f_{*}\colon\mathbf{Shv}(X_{{l\acute{e}t}})\to\mathbf{Shv}(Y_{{l\acute{e}t}})
is a geometric morphism of topoi,
and hence
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(X_{{l\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{S})\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{hv}(Y_{{l\acute{e}t}},\mathcal{S})
is a geometric morphism of
\infty
-topoi.
Combined with (
9.2.2
),
we define the functor

(9.2.4)

\Pi^{{l\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{lSch}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})

by sending
X\in\mathbf{lSch}
to the shape
\Pi^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X_{{l\acute{e}t}})
.
Composing with the localization functor we obtain the functor

(9.2.5)

\Pi_{\ell}^{l\acute{e}t}\colon\mathbf{lSch}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}.

Lemma 9.2.6
.

Let
U_{\bullet}\to X
be a log étale hypercover in
\mathbf{lSch}
.
Then there is an equivalence of pro-spaces

\Pi^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X)\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}_{i}(\Pi^{{l\acute{e}t}}(U_{i})).

Proof.

This is a special case of the claim in
[
47
, Remark 1.6]
.
The proof uses descent and the fact that (
9.2.2
) preserves small colimits.
∎

The functor
\Pi_{\ell}^{l\acute{e}t}
in (
9.2.5
) satisfies
\square
-invariance by Theorem
9.1.5
,
Proposition
9.1.6
, and (
9.2.3
).
Moreover,

\Pi_{\ell}^{l\acute{e}t}
satisfies log étale descent due to Lemma
9.2.6
.
Hence by Proposition
3.4.1
,
for every
S\in\mathbf{lSch}
,
we obtain a functor

(9.2.6)

\mathrm{Re}_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{{l\acute{e}t}}(S)\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}

sending
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
to
\Pi_{\ell}^{l\acute{e}t}(X)
.
We refer to
\mathrm{Re}_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}
as the
unstable
\ell
-adic log étale realization functor
.

Lemma 9.2.7
.

Suppose
k
is a separably closed field of characteristic
p
and
\ell\neq p
.
For every
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
and integer
i
, there is a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups

H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\cong H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(X-\partial X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell).

Proof.

Combine Proposition
9.1.6
and
[
50
, Corollary 7.5]
.
∎

Lemma 9.2.8
.

Suppose
k
is a separably closed field of characteristic
p
and
\ell\neq p
.
For every
X,Y\in\mathbf{lSm}/k
, there exists a canonical
equivalence in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}

\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X\times_{k}Y)\simeq\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X)\times\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(Y).

Proof.

We have isomorphisms of graded rings

(9.2.7)

\begin{split}H^{*}(\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X)\times\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(Y),\mathbb{Z}/\ell)&\cong H^{*}(\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X),\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}H^{*}(\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(Y),\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\\
&\cong H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{*}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\\
&\cong H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X-\partial X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(Y-\partial Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell),\end{split}

where the first isomorphism comes from the Künneth formula in algebraic topology, the second isomorphism comes from (
9.2.3
), and the third isomorphism comes from Lemma
9.2.7
.

We also have isomorphisms of graded rings

(9.2.8)

\begin{split}H^{*}(\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(X\times_{k}Y),\mathbb{Z}/\ell)&\cong H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X\times_{k}Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\\
&\cong H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X-\partial X\times_{k}Y-\partial Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\end{split}

where the first isomorphism comes from (
9.2.3
), and the second isomorphism comes Lemma
9.2.7
.
The Künneth formula

\mathbb{R}\Gamma_{{\acute{e}t}}(X-\partial X\times_{k}Y-\partial Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\simeq\mathbb{R}\Gamma_{{\acute{e}t}}(X-\partial X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Gamma_{{\acute{e}t}}(Y-\partial Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)

in
[
33
, Th. finitude, Corollaire 1.11]
gives an isomorphism of graded rings

(9.2.9)

H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X-\partial X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(Y-\partial Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\cong H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{*}(X-\partial X\times_{k}Y-\partial Y,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)

since
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
is a field.

Combine (
9.2.7
), (
9.2.8
), and (
9.2.9
) to conclude the desired equivalence.
∎

If
k
is a separably closed field of characteristic
p
and
\ell\neq p
,
we have the
\ell
-adic Tate module
T_{\ell}\mu:=\mathop{\rm lim}_{r}\mu_{\ell^{r}}
.
There is an isomorphism of pro-groups
T_{\ell}\mu\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
.
In
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
,
we have the equivalent mixed spheres

S_{\ell}^{1,0}:=K(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell},1)\text{ and }S_{\ell}^{1,1}:=K(T_{\ell}\mu,1),

where
K(-,1)
denotes the Eilenberg-MacLane space.
The naturally induced functor
\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
sends
S^{1}
to
S_{\ell}^{1,0}
.
Hence
S_{\ell}^{1,0}
is invertible in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{H\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
.
It follows that
S_{\ell}^{1,1}
is invertible in

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{H\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
too.

Lemma 9.2.9
.

Suppose
k
is a separably closed field of characteristic
p
and
\ell\neq p
.
In
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
,
there is an equivalence

\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}})/\Pi_{p}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{pt})\simeq S_{\ell}^{1,1}.

Proof.

By combining
[
50
, Corollary 7.5]
and Proposition
9.1.6
we obtain the computation of
log étale cohomology groups

(9.2.10)

H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{Z}/\ell&\text{if }i=0,1,\\
0&\text{if }i>1.\end{array}\right.

The fs log scheme
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}
has a Zariski cover consisting of the two patches

U_{1}:=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}x\to\mathbb{Z}[x])\text{ and }U_{2}:=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{N}(1/x)\to\mathbb{Z}[1/x]).

For every integer
r\geq 1
, there is a morphism

m_{r}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}

given by the formula
x\mapsto x^{\ell^{r}}
.
The assumption
\ell\neq p
implies that
m_{\ell^{r}}
is Kummer étale for every integer
r\geq 1
.
The connected component of the Čech nerve associated with
m_{r}
is
\mathrm{B}\mu_{\ell^{r}}
.
Hence we have a morphism of pro-spaces

\alpha_{\ell^{r}}\colon\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}})\to\mathrm{B}\mu_{\ell^{r}}.

One can readily check that the naturally induced homomorphism of abelian groups

(9.2.11)

\alpha_{\ell^{r}}^{*}\colon H^{i}(\mathrm{B}\mu_{\ell^{r}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\to H^{i}(\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}),\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\cong H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell)

is an isomorphism for
i=0,1
.
Collecting
\alpha_{\ell^{r}}
for all
r\geq 1
we produce a morphism of pro-spaces

\beta_{\ell}\colon\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}})\to\mathop{\rm lim}_{r}\mathrm{B}\mu_{\ell^{r}}.

The quotient homomorphism
\mu_{\ell^{r+1}}\to\mu_{\ell^{r}}
naturally induces a homomorphism

H^{i}(\mathrm{B}\mu_{\ell^{r}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell)\to H^{i}(\mathrm{B}\mu_{\ell^{r+1}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell)

for every integer
i\geq 0
.
This is an isomorphism for
i=0,1
and the zero map for every integer
i>1
, see
[
90
, Exercise 1, §XIII.1]
.
Since (
9.2.11
) is an isomorphism for
i=0,1
, we deduce that
\beta_{\ell}
is an equivalence in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
together with the computation (
9.2.10
).
∎

Suppose
k
is a separably closed field of characteristic
p
and
\ell\neq p
.
The composite

\mathbf{lSm}/k\xrightarrow{\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{H\mathbb{Z}/\ell}

is symmetric monoidal by Lemma
9.2.8
,
and it sends
1\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
to a map in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{H\mathbb{Z}/\ell}
whose cofiber is an invertible object by

\square
-invariance of
\Pi_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}
and Lemma
9.2.9
.
Hence Proposition
3.4.3
gives a functor

(9.2.12)

\mathrm{Re}_{\ell}^{{l\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{{l\acute{e}t}}(k)\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{H\mathbb{Z}/\ell}

of
\infty
-categories, which we call the
stable
\ell
-adic realization functor
.

Lemma 9.2.10
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a log étale morphism of fs log schemes.
If there is a strict morphism
X\to\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}
,
then
f
is Kummer étale.

Proof.

The question is étale local on
Y
,
so owing to
[
78
, Theorem IV.3.3.1]
we may assume
f
admits a chart
\theta\colon\mathbb{N}\to P

with the following properties:

\theta
is injective,
the cokernel of
\theta^{{\mathrm{gp}}}
is finite,
and the naturally induced morphism
g\colon Y\to X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}\mathbb{A}_{P}
is strict étale.

An elementary argument shows that for every
x\in P
,
there exists an integer
n>0
such that
nx
is in the image of
\theta
.
In other words,
\theta
is Kummer.
Hence the projection
p\colon X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{N}}}\mathbb{A}_{P}\to X
is Kummer étale.
Since
g
is strict étale,
f=pg
is Kummer étale.
∎

Lemma 9.2.11
.

Suppose
k
is a separably closed field of characteristic
p>0
.
In
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/p}
, there is an equivalence

\Pi_{p}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}})/\Pi_{p}^{{l\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{pt})\simeq 0.

Proof.

We need to check that

H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathbb{Z}/p)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbb{Z}/p&\text{if }i=0,\\
0&\text{if }i>0.\end{array}\right.

The Artin-Schreier sequence

0\to\mathbb{Z}/p\to\mathbb{G}_{a}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}\to 0

gives a long exact sequence

\cdots\to H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathbb{Z}/p)\to H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}})\to H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}})\to H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i+1}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathbb{Z}/p)\to\cdots.

Hence it remains to check the vanishing
H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}})=0
for
i>0
.
Lemma
9.2.10
implies an isomorphism of cohomology groups

H_{{l\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}})\cong H_{{k\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}})

for every integer
i\geq 0
.
Furthermore,

[
56
, Proposition 6.5]
(see also
[
77
, Proposition 3.27]
) implies an
isomorphism of abelian groups

H_{{k\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\mathrm{log}}})\cong H_{{\acute{e}t}}^{i}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})

for every integer
i\geq 0
.
A classical computation of the Zariski cohomology of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and the comparison between Zariski
and étale cohomology show that the last group vanishes for
i>0
.
∎

Suppose
k
is a separably closed field of characteristic
p>0
.
Due to Lemma
9.2.11
,
we
cannot
apply Proposition
3.4.3
to the composite

\mathbf{lSm}/k\xrightarrow{\Pi_{p}^{{l\acute{e}t}}}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{Z}/p}\to\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{H\mathbb{Z}/p}.

This suggests that one may need to enlarge
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{ro}(\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt})^{H\mathbb{Z}/p}
in order to construct a
stable
p
-adic realization from
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}_{{l\acute{e}t}}(k)
.

9.3.
Kato-Nakayama realizations

Throughout this subsection,
we fix a field
k
of characteristic
0
and an embedding
k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

There is a functor to the category of topological spaces

(9.3.1)

(-)^{\log}\colon\mathbf{lSch}/k\to\mathbf{Spc}

sending
X\in\mathbf{lSch}/k
to its Kato-Nakayama space
X^{\log}:=(X\times_{k}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}))^{\log}
.
We refer to
[
57
, §1]
for details.
This naturally induces a functor to the
\infty
-category of spaces

(9.3.2)

(-)^{\log}\colon\mathbf{lSch}/k\to\mathcal{S}

If
P
is an fs monoid,
we set

\mathrm{R}_{P}:=\operatorname{Hom}(P,\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})\text{ and }\mathrm{T}_{P}:=\operatorname{Hom}(P,S^{1}),

where the monoid operations on
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
S^{1}
are the multiplications.
We can view
\mathrm{R}_{P}
and
\mathrm{T}_{P}
as topological spaces.
According to
[
57
, Example 1.2.1.1]
,
there is a canonical homeomorphism

(9.3.3)

(\mathbb{A}_{P})^{\log}\cong\mathrm{R}_{P}\times\mathrm{T}_{P}.

Proposition 9.3.1
.

Suppose
f\colon X\to Z
and
g\colon Y\to Z
are morphisms in
\mathbf{lSch}/k
.
If either
f
or
g
is exact, then there is a canonical homeomorphism

(X\times_{Z}Y)^{\log}\cong X^{\log}\times_{Z^{\log}}Y^{\log}.

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
78
, Proposition V.1.2.11]
.
∎

Proposition 9.3.2
.

Suppose
X\in\mathbf{lSch}/k
.
Then there are canonical homotopy equivalences

(X\times\square)^{\log}\simeq(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\log}\simeq X^{\log}

Proof.

Owing to Proposition
9.3.1
,
we reduce to the case when
X=k
.
By (
9.3.3
),
there are homeomorphisms

(\mathbb{P}^{1}-0,\infty)^{\log}\cong\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1},\;(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\log}\cong\mathbb{R}^{2},\text{ and }(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\log}\cong\mathbb{R}_{>0}\times S^{1}.

In particular,
(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\log}
is contractible.
From the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathbb{G}}_{m})^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{P}^{1}-0,\infty)^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square^{\log}}

we see that
\square^{\log}
is homeomorphic to
[0,1]^{2}
,
where we regard
[0,1]
as a compactification of
(0,1)\cong\mathbb{R}
.
This shows that
\square^{\log}
is contractible too.
∎

A continuous map
f\colon X\to Y
of spaces is called
étale
(resp. an étale cover)
if
f
is a local homeomorphism (resp. surjective local homeomorphism).

Proposition 9.3.3
.

Suppose
f\colon\mathscr{X}\to X
is a Kummer étale hypercover.
Then the naturally induced map
f^{\log}\colon\mathscr{X}^{\log}\to X^{\log}
is an étale hypercover.

Proof.

Combine
[
57
, Lemma 2.2]
and Proposition
9.3.1
.
∎

Lemma 9.3.4
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a dividing cover of fs log schemes.
If
X
is quasi-compact and has a chart
P
such that
P
is sharp, then there exists a subdivision
\Sigma\to\operatorname{Spec}(P)
of toric fans such that the naturally induced morphism

Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\to X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}

is an isomorphism of fs log schemes.

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
22
, Proposition A.11.5]
.
∎

Proposition 9.3.5
.

Suppose
f\colon Y\to X
is a dividing cover in
\mathbf{lSch}/k
.
Then the naturally induced map

f^{\log}\colon Y^{\log}\to X^{\log}

is an equivalence of spaces.

Proof.

We only need to consider the case when
X
is connected.
Then
f^{\log}
is a proper surjective map of connected finite CW-complexes.
By
[
91
]
,
it suffices to show that
(f^{\log})^{-1}(x)
is contractible for every
x\in X^{\log}
.
This question is Zariski local on
X
,
so we may assume that
X
admits a chart
X\to\mathbb{A}_{P}
,
where
P
is sharp,
see
[
78
, Proposition II.2.3.7]
.
According to Lemma
9.3.4
,
there exists a subdivision
\Sigma\to\operatorname{Spec}(P)
of toric fans such that the naturally induced morphism

Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\to X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}

is an isomorphism.
Hence it suffices to show that both maps

(Y\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma})^{\log}\to Y^{\log}\text{ and }(X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma})^{\log}\to X^{\log}

are equivalences of spaces.
This means that we only need to consider the case when the morphism
f
is the projection
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\to X

for some morphism
u\colon X\to\mathbb{A}_{P}
and subdivision
\Sigma\to\operatorname{Spec}(P)
.

Since this question is Zariski local on
X
,
we may assume that
u
factors through a chart
X\to\mathbb{A}_{Q}
such that
Q
is sharp and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}\cong Q
,
see
[
78
, Proposition II.2.3.7]
.
Since
\operatorname{Spec}(Q)\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(P)}\Sigma\to\operatorname{Spec}(Q)
is again a subdivision,
we may reduce to the following case:
The morphism
f
is the projection
X\times_{\mathbb{A}_{P}}\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\to X
for some chart
X\to\mathbb{A}_{P}
and subdivision

\Sigma\to\operatorname{Spec}(P)
, where
P
is sharp and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,x}\cong P
.
Now,
by
[
57
, Lemma 1.3]
,
there is a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma})^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}_{P})^{\log}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\log}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\log}}

Here
g\colon\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\to\mathbb{A}_{P}
is the naturally induced morphism.
Hence we are reduced to the case when
f
is the morphism
\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma}\to\mathbb{A}_{P}
and
x
is the origin of
(\mathbb{A}_{P})^{\log}
.
In this case,
let
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}
be the cones of
\Sigma
.
Then
f
is locally given by
f_{i}\colon\mathbb{T}_{\sigma_{i}^{\wedge}}\to\mathbb{A}_{P}
.
Hence the morphism
f^{\log}
is locally given by the product of the naturally induced maps

\mathrm{R}_{f_{i}}\colon\mathrm{R}_{\sigma_{i}^{\vee}}\to\mathrm{R}_{P}
and
\mathrm{T}_{f_{i}}\colon\mathrm{T}_{\sigma_{i}^{\vee}}\to\mathrm{T}_{P}
.

The multiplication map
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
given by
(x,y)\mapsto xy

naturally induces a commutative square

(9.3.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{\sigma_{i}^{\vee}}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{\sigma_{i}^{\vee}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{P}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{P}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{f_{i}}\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{f_{i}}}

Since
\mathrm{T}_{f_{i}}
is a homeomorphism, (
9.3.4
) gives a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}_{\sigma_{i}^{\vee}})^{\log}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}_{\sigma_{i}^{\vee}})^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}_{P})^{\log}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}_{P})^{\log}.}

By gluing these squares together we obtain the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma})^{\log}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}_{\Sigma})^{\log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}_{P})^{\log}\times[0,1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{A}_{P})^{\log}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\log}}

As a consequence,
we obtain a map
h\colon(g^{\log})^{-1}(0)\times[0,1]\to(g^{\log})^{-1}(0)
such that
h(u,0)=0

and
h(u,1)=u
for all
u
.
This shows that
(g^{\log})^{-1}(0)
is contractible.
∎

Propositions
9.3.2
,
9.3.3
, and
9.3.5
show
the Kato-Nakayama realization
(-)^{\log}
is
\square
-invariant and satisfies
dNis
-descent.
Hence by Proposition
3.4.1
, we obtain a functor

(9.3.5)

\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{KN}}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)\to\mathcal{S}

sending
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
to
X^{\log}
for every
S\in\mathbf{lSch}/k
and
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
.
We refer to
\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{KN}}
as the

unstable Kato-Nakayama realization functor
.
The constant presheaf functor
\mathcal{S}\to\mathbf{Psh}(\mathbf{lSm}/S)
naturally induces a functor
\mathcal{S}\to\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(S)
.
This is a section of (
9.3.5
).
Similarly,
in the pointed setting,
have a functor

(9.3.6)

\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{KN}}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)\to\mathcal{S}_{\ast}.

It also admits a section.

Due to Proposition
9.3.1
, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}_{\ast}(S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes S^{2}}

commutes.
Taking colimits in
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{L}}
and using (
2.5.7
) we obtain a functor

(9.3.7)

\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{KN}}\colon\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(S)\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}.

We refer to
\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{KN}}
as the
stable Kato-Nakayama realization functor
.
It also admits a section.
It sends the suspension spectrum
\Sigma_{T}^{\infty}X_{+}
to
\Sigma_{S^{2}}^{\infty}(X^{\log})_{+}
for every
X\in\mathbf{lSm}/S
.

Suppose
S\in\mathbf{lSch}/k
has the chart
\mathbb{N}
or
0
.
Then every morphism
f\colon X\to S
in
\mathbf{lSch}/k
is exact, see
[
78
, Proposition I.4.2.1(4)]
.
Hence, by Proposition
9.3.1
, the functor

(-)^{\log}\colon\mathbf{lSm}/S\to\mathcal{S}

is symmetric monoidal.
This implies that the enhanced Kato-Nakayama realization functors (
9.3.5
), (
9.3.6
),
and (
9.3.7
) are symmetric monoidal.

Proposition 9.3.6
.

There is a commutative triangle

(9.3.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\mathcal{H}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{KN}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{Betti}}}

where
\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{Betti}}
denotes the Betti realization functor

\mathcal{H}(k)\to\mathcal{S}
.
There is a similar commutative triangle for
\mathrm{log}\mathcal{SH}(k)
,
\mathcal{SH}(k)
, and
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{pt}
.

Proof.

According to
[
78
, Theorem V.1.3.1]
,

X^{\log}
is a manifold with boundary,
and its interior is the Betti realization
(X-\partial X)^{\mathrm{Betti}}
of
X-\partial X
.
The collar neighborhood theorem
[
27
, Theorem 2]
implies that the inclusion

(X-\partial X)^{\mathrm{Betti}}\to X^{\log}

is a homotopy equivalence.
This means that the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{lSm}/S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\mapsto X-\partial X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\mapsto X^{\log}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Re}_{\mathrm{Betti}}}

commutes.
Together with Proposition
3.4.1
, we deduce that (
9.3.8
) commutes.

We can similarly show that the pointed version of (
9.3.8
) commutes.
For the stable version,
we use (
2.5.7
) and (
4.0.5
).
∎

Appendix A
Cubes and total cofibers

To efficiently deal with iterated cofibers,
we will introduce the notion of the total cofibers of
n
-cubes in this subsection.
We will use this notion in Theorem
8.4.4
.

Definition A.0.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a pointed
\infty
-category with small colimits.
For an integer
n\geq 0
, an
n
-cube
in
\mathcal{C}
is a map

Q\colon(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathcal{C},

compare
[
64
, Definition 6.1.1.2]
.
An
n
-cubical horn
in
\mathcal{C}
is a map

Q\colon(\Delta^{1})^{n}-\{(1,\ldots,1)\}\to\mathcal{C}.

For every
n
-cube
Q
, we can naturally associate an
n
-cubical horn denoted by

\mathrm{horn}(Q)
.
Moreover,
we set
\mathrm{vertex}(Q):=Q(1,\ldots,1)
.

The cofiber construction applies to
1
-cubes.
We give a generalization to
n
-cubes as follows.

Definition A.0.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a pointed
\infty
-category with small colimits.
An
n
-cube
Q
in
\mathcal{C}
naturally induces a map

\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q))\to\mathrm{vertex}(Q).

The
total cofiber
of
Q
,
denoted by
\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)
,
is the cofiber of this map.
Thus there is a cofiber sequence

(A.0.1)

\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q))\to\mathrm{vertex}(Q)\to\operatorname{tcofib}(Q).

If
Q
is a
1
-cube, then
\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)=\operatorname{cofib}(Q)
.
We say that
Q
is
cocartesian
if there is an equivalence
\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\simeq 0
in
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition A.0.3
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a pointed
\infty
-category with limits.
An
n
-cube in
\mathcal{C}
naturally induces a map

Q(0,\ldots,0)\to\mathop{\rm lim}(Q|_{(\Delta^{1})^{n}-\{0,\ldots,0\}}).

The
total fiber
of
Q
,
denoted by
\operatorname{tfib}(Q)
,
is the fiber of this map.
We say that
Q
is
cartesian
if there is an equivalence
\operatorname{tfib}(Q)\simeq 0
in
\mathcal{C}
.

Note that if
Q
is a
1
-cube, then
\operatorname{tfib}(Q)=\operatorname{fib}(Q)
.

Proposition A.0.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a pointed
\infty
-category with small colimits.
For every
n
-cube
Q\colon(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathcal{C}
and integer
1\leq i\leq n
,
there is a canonically induced cocartesian square

(A.0.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q|_{(\Delta^{1})^{i-1}\times\{0\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-i}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q|_{(\Delta^{1})^{i-1}\times\{1\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-i}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{vertex}(Q|_{(\Delta^{1})^{i-1}\times\{0\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q)).}

Proof.

For simplicity of notation, we may assume
i=1
.
We set

A_{i}:=\{i\}\times\Delta^{n-1},\;B_{i}:=\{i\}\times(\Delta^{n-1}-\{(1,\ldots,1)\})

for
i=0,1
and

C:=\Delta^{1}\times(\Delta^{n-1}-\{(1,\ldots,1)\})\;D:=\Delta^{n}-\{(1,\ldots,1)\}.

Since
A_{0}
has a final object, we have an equivalence in
\mathcal{C}

(A.0.3)

\mathrm{vertex}(Q|_{A_{0}})\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}(Q|_{A_{0}}).

The inclusion
\{1\}\to\Delta^{1}
is right anodyne, so it is final due to
[
63
, Corollary 4.1.1.12]
.
Together with
[
63
, Corollary 4.1.2.7]
, we deduce that the inclusion
B_{1}\to C
is final.
It follows that we have an equivalence in
\mathcal{C}

(A.0.4)

\mathop{\rm colim}(Q|_{B_{1}})\simeq\mathop{\rm colim}(Q|_{C}).

The square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}

is cocartesian.
Apply
[
63
, Proposition 4.4.2.2]
to deduce that the square

(A.0.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(Q|_{B_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(Q|_{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(Q|_{A_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(Q|_{D})}

is cocartesian.
Together with (
A.0.3
) and (
A.0.4
), we see that (
A.0.2
) is cocartesian.
∎

Proposition A.0.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category with small colimits.
For every
n
-cube
Q\colon(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathcal{C}
and integer
1\leq i\leq n
,
there is a canonically induced cofiber sequence

(A.0.6)

\operatorname{tcofib}(Q|_{(\Delta^{1})^{i-1}\times\{0\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-i}})\to\operatorname{tcofib}(Q|_{(\Delta^{1})^{i-1}\times\{1\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-i}})\to\operatorname{tcofib}(Q).

Proof.

We set
Q_{j}:=Q|_{(\Delta^{1})^{i-1}\times\{j\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-i}}
for
j=0,1
and

\mathcal{F}:=\operatorname{cofib}(\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q_{0}))\to\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q))).

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q_{1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{vertex}(Q_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathrm{horn}(Q))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{vertex}(Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tcofib}(Q_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tcofib}(Q).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(b)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d)}

By definition,
(b)
,
(b)\cup(c)
, and
(c)\cup(d)
are cocartesian.
Proposition
A.0.4
shows that
(a)
is cocartesian.
Use the pasting law to deduce that
(a)\cup(b)
is cocartesian.
This implies there is an equivalence in
\mathcal{C}

(A.0.7)

\mathcal{F}\simeq\operatorname{tcofib}(Q_{0}).

Use the pasting law two times to deduce that
(d)
is cocartesian.
Together with (
A.0.7
), we have the desired cofiber sequence.
∎

Proposition A.0.6
.

Let
Q\colon(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathcal{C}
be an
n
-cube in a pointed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with small colimits,
and let
X
be an object of
\mathcal{C}
.
If the tensor product operation on
\mathcal{C}
preserves small colimits in each variable, then there is a canonical equivalence in
\mathcal{C}

\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\otimes X\simeq\operatorname{tcofib}(Q^{\prime}),

where
Q^{\prime}
is the naturally associated
n
-cube sending any point
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
to
Q(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\otimes X
.

Proof.

Apply the assumption that the tensor product operation preserves small colimits in each variable to (
A.0.1
).
∎

Proposition A.0.7
.

Let
Q\colon(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathcal{C}
be an
n
-cube in a pointed symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with small colimits,
and let
i\colon X_{1}\to X_{0}
be a map in
\mathcal{C}
.
If the tensor product operation on
\mathcal{C}
preserves small colimits in each variable, then there is a canonical equivalence in
\mathcal{C}

\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\otimes\operatorname{cofib}(f)\simeq\operatorname{tcofib}(Q^{\prime}),

where
Q^{\prime}
is the naturally associated
(n+1)
-cube sending any point
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n+1})
to

Q(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\otimes X_{a_{n+1}}
.

Proof.

We set

Q_{i}^{\prime}:=Q^{\prime}|_{(\Delta^{1})^{n}\times\{i\}}

for
i=0,1
.
Owing to Proposition
A.0.6
, there is a commutative square in
\mathcal{C}
with vertical equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\otimes X_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\otimes X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tcofib}(Q_{0}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tcofib}(Q_{1}^{\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Since the tensor product operation preserves small colimits, we have a cofiber sequence

\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\otimes X_{0}\to\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\otimes X_{1}\to\operatorname{tcofib}(Q)\otimes\operatorname{cofib}(f).

Combine with (
A.0.6
) to conclude.
∎

Proposition A.0.8
.

Let
i_{1}\colon X_{01}\to X_{11}
,
\ldots
,
i_{n}\colon X_{0n}\to X_{1n}
be maps in a pointed symmetric monoidal

\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with small colimits.
If the tensor product operation on
\mathcal{C}
preserves small colimits in each variable,
then there is a canonical equivalence in
\mathcal{C}

\operatorname{cofib}(i_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\operatorname{cofib}(i_{n})\simeq\operatorname{tcofib}(Q_{i_{1}\cdots i_{n}}).

Here we write
Q
for the naturally associated
n
-cube sending any point
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
to

X_{a_{1}1}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{a_{n}n}
.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
A.0.7
repeatedly.
∎

Definition A.0.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category with fiber products.
For any object
X\in\mathcal{C}
,
the
cube associated with a family of maps
\{U_{i}\to X\}_{1\leq i\leq n}

is the
n
-cube

(\Delta^{1})^{n}\to\mathcal{C}

sending a vertex of the form
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
to
Y_{a_{1}1}\times_{X}\cdots\times_{X}Y_{a_{n}n}
,
where
Y_{0i}:=U_{i}
and
Y_{1i}:=X
.
The
cubical horn associated with
\{U_{i}\to X\}_{1\leq i\leq n}

is the restriction of the above cube to
(\Delta^{1})^{n}-\{(1,\ldots,1)\}
.
For example, if
n=2
, then the cube associated with
U_{1}\to X
and
U_{2}\to X
is the
2
-cube

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{1}\times U_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}

Any stable
\infty
-category has small colimits and limits, see
[
64
, Proposition 1.1.3.4]
.

Proposition A.0.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category.
Then an
n
-cube
Q
in
\mathcal{C}
is cartesian if and only if it is cocartesian.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n
.
The claim is clear if
n=1
.
Suppose
n>1
.
We set
Q_{i}:=Q|_{\{i\}\times(\Delta^{1})^{n-1}}
for
i=0,1
.
Owing to
[
64
, Proposition 1.1.3.1]
, the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Fun}((\Delta^{1})^{n},\mathcal{C})
is stable.
Furthermore, the
(n-1)
-cubes
\operatorname{cofib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1})
and
\operatorname{fib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1})
can be computed pointwise.
Since
\mathcal{C}
is stable, we have an equivalence in
\mathcal{C}

\operatorname{cofib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1})\simeq\Sigma\operatorname{fib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1}),

where
\Sigma
is the suspension functor.
In particular,
\operatorname{cofib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1})
is cocartesian if and only if
\operatorname{fib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1})
is cartesian by induction.

Owing to (
A.0.6
),
Q
is cocartesian if and only if
\operatorname{cofib}(\operatorname{tcofib}(Q_{0})\to\operatorname{tcofib}(Q_{1}))\simeq 0
,
which is equivalent to
\operatorname{tcofib}(\operatorname{cofib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1}))\simeq 0
.
This is equivalent to saying that
\operatorname{cofib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1})
is cocartesian.
We can similarly show that
Q
is cartesian if and only if
\operatorname{fib}(Q_{0}\to Q_{1})
is cartesian.
This finishes the proof.
∎
